# FIXES — Civitics Platform

Actionable improvement backlog. Every item has a priority, complexity, and enough context to hand to Qwen or Claude Code directly.

**Priority key:**
- 🔴 Critical — Bug that breaks or blocks real functionality
- 🟠 High — Meaningful product/UX gap, address soon
- 🟡 Medium — Worthwhile improvement, schedule when practical
- 🟢 Quick Win — Small effort, high visible impact (batch these)
- ⬜ Future — Phase 2+ or requires significant design/pipeline work

**Complexity key:** S = <2h · M = 2–8h · L = 1–3 days · XL = multi-day + planning

**Workflow:** Every bullet has a stable ID (`<!--id:FIX-NNN-->`). Don't remove or renumber IDs — they're the handle commits reference via `Fixes: FIX-NNN` trailers.

**This file is hand-edited only (FIX-1016).** It holds prose and the ID marker. It carries **no status** — no `[ ]`, no `[x]`. Completion lives in `docs/done.log`, which is append-only, and is *derived* from it:

> **OPEN** if the ID has no row in `done.log`, or if its **last** row has `reopen` in the sha column. **CLOSED** otherwise.

Ask the question with **`pnpm fixes:status FIX-NNN`** (or `pnpm fixes:status` for the whole open backlog, `--json` for a machine). `pnpm session:check` prints the same summary at the top of every session. See [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md#fixes-workflow) for the full contract.

**Bullet shape:** `- {emoji} {S|M|L|XL} — **title** — body <!--id:FIX-NNN-->`. Use `pnpm fix:add` rather than hand-formatting; it allocates the next free ID atomically and writes this shape.

**Section rules:**
- Active sections hold open items. `pnpm fixes:clean` moves closed ones to `## COMPLETED`; `pnpm fixes:archive` moves those out to `docs/archive/fixes-archive.md`. Both are run deliberately by a human, never as a side effect of a sync.
- `## COMPLETED` is a holding pen between those two commands — normally empty.
- Deferred / blocked items stay in active sections, never in COMPLETED. If something was closed prematurely, `pnpm fix:reopen FIX-NNN --note "why"` (or a `Reopens: FIX-NNN` commit trailer) appends a `reopen` row and the derivation does the rest. Nothing in this file changes.
- Bullets in `docs/archive/fixes-archive.md` still show `[x]`. That is history, written before the strip, and is left exactly as it was.

---

## STRATEGIC PILLARS
> Directional goals, not checkable tasks. Concrete sub-tasks are threaded throughout this doc. Phase 2+ strategy, architecture, and the Social App live in `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

## BUGS — Fix These First
- 🟠 S — **get_financial_entity_naics reads from_id but every NAICS-bearing contract is agency->entity — the NAICS pass has written zero rows since inception** — **Filed, not fixed** — found while verifying [[FIX-910]], out of scope for the [[FIX-916]] curation PR because repointing it WRITES thousands of new industry tags on prod, which is a data change needing its own before/after. `get_financial_entity_naics()` (supabase/migrations/20260530020000_financial_size_tags_serverside_443.sql) aggregates `fr.from_id AS entity_id ... WHERE fr.from_type = 'financial_entity' AND fr.relationship_type IN ('contract','grant') AND fr.metadata->>'naics_code' IS NOT NULL`. But USASpending writes contracts in the **agency to recipient** direction: measured on prod 2026-07-28, `financial_relationships` holds 3,240,205 `agency -> financial_entity` contract rows of which **3,238,246 carry a `naics_code`**, plus 582,485 `agency -> financial_entity` grant rows with none, plus exactly 54 `financial_entity -> financial_entity` grant rows — also with none. So the contractor whose NAICS code we want is `to_id`, and the function's `from_type='financial_entity'` filter matches only those 54 naics-less grant rows. **`get_financial_entity_naics()` returns `[]` on BOTH envs**, and `SELECT count(*) FROM entity_tags WHERE entity_type='financial_entity' AND tag_category='industry' AND metadata ? 'naics_code'` is **0 on prod**. The entire NAICS half of `tagFinancialEntities()` is dead code that has never emitted a tag. Consequences: (1) the ~78 contractor rows the [[FIX-909]] NAICS2_INDUSTRY sector corrections were written to protect do not exist — those corrections are still right, they are just currently unreachable; (2) the function scans the contract/grant slice of a 3.8M-row table every nightly to produce an empty array; (3) US contractors get no industry label from their own federal contract industry code, only from name-keyword guessing. Fix is to aggregate `fr.to_id` where `to_type='financial_entity'` (keep the `from_id` arm too if the 54-row financial_entity-sourced grant path is ever populated). **Size the blast radius first** — count distinct `to_id` with a naics_code and diff the resulting tag set against today's keyword tags before shipping, because these rows land at confidence 0.85/primary and will collide with keyword tags on the `(entity_type, entity_id, tag, tag_category)` unique constraint. Cross-ref [[FIX-910]] [[FIX-909]] [[FIX-443]] [[FIX-916]]. <!--id:FIX-919-->
- 🔴 M — **FEC recipient-set shrinkage has no retraction: 209k stale cycle-2026 FR rows / $1.48B double-counted on 3,416 recipients** — MEASURED on prod 2026-08-25 after the PR 3b full replay completed (nightly 32803399130, fec_bulk 03:06-03:36 UTC, run_state cleared). The cycle-2026 indiv slice (metadata->>source in fec_bulk_indiv, fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee) holds 2,203,015 rows / $5,278,268,033 against the FIX-1068 harness expectation of 1,980,786 / $4,556,216,174 (audit 2026-08-18-fec-coverage-pr3a-phase0.md 7.1) — a residue of 222,229 rows / $722.1M, and the harness figure is an UPPER bound for prod because the harness emits to every ccl P/A candidate while the pipeline emits only to matched officials. DECOMPOSITION (instrument: updated_at bucket x small_dollar_bracket_rollup membership; the 08-25 run REPLACED every cycle-2026 bracket row for every recipient it resolved, so bracket-row presence is a prod-side proxy for emit-set membership that needs no harness re-run). Classify each (to_type,to_id): A never touched by the replay / B fully rewritten / C partially touched. A-with-no-bracket-row = 3,416 recipients, 209,017 rows, $1,481,928,516 — never resolved by the run at all. A-with-bracket-row = 275 recipients / 7,818 rows — in the emit set, merely dormant, NOT residue. So ~94% of the 222,229-row residue is one population: recipients that dropped out of the emit set and whose prior rows nothing retracts. Split: 2,736 financial_entity recipients (136,072 rows, $1,361,948,580) + 680 OFFICIALS (72,945 rows, $119,979,936). False-positive bound ~2%: among class-C recipients (provably in the emit set) 806/4,605 = 17.5% lack a bracket row, which implies only ~58 of the 3,416 could be in-set-but-bracketless. NOT a PR 3b artifact — the stale rows last-wrote across 2026-05-03 to 2026-08-17 (biggest cohorts: 488 officials/52,315 rows on 07-27; 527 entities/103,459 rows on 06-28), so this has accumulated over every run since the pipeline existed. 3b only made it legible by moving the totals. MECHANISM REFUTED: the cc-87 prompt's leading hypothesis was donor fingerprint canonicalisation drift producing duplicate from_ids beside stale rows. financial_entities carries a UNIQUE index on donor_fingerprint (financial_entities_donor_fingerprint_unique) and there is NO merged_into column, and Ossoff (1376dc1e-f697-40b2-8c0f-780f8fe8ea00) reproduces the harness EXACTLY — 38,973 FR rows / $25,844,495 and sub-floor 29,551 / $2,778,748 — i.e. zero residue on a large recipient. The residue is per-RECIPIENT, not per-donor. WHO READS THE SLICE: officials.total_received_cents, official_donor_totals, the donor rollup MV, treemap brackets and the graph money branch all sum this slice, so the $119,979,936 on 680 officials is live double-counted money on official pages today. SWEEP SHAPE (build after Craig sizes it, do NOT run blind): (1) the indiv writer records every (cycle, source, to_type, to_id, from_id) key it emits into a run-scoped staging table; (2) after a run that reports COMPLETE for the cycle, an anti-join over the (cycle, source) slice marks or DELETEs rows absent from the emitted-key set, Craig-gated, dry-run count first; (3) FIX-943 vacuum tail on financial_relationships after. STANDING RULE this establishes: a semantics-changing or recipient-set-changing full replay must name its retraction mechanism before it runs — an upsert-only replay cannot shrink a set, and every prior full replay in this pipeline silently assumed it could. Cross-ref FIX-1068 (the replay), FIX-1008 (the IS DISTINCT FROM guard that makes updated_at a partial instrument), FIX-943 (vacuum tail), FIX-754 (resume). - 🟡 S — **TIGER district upsert never writes centroid — a refresh leaves every district's centroid at its old position** — upsert_district_jurisdiction() (supabase/migrations/20260428061525_district_boundaries.sql) sets boundary_geometry on both its INSERT and UPDATE paths and NEVER touches centroid. The 7211 district rows all have a centroid today only because the one-shot backfill migration 20260528180100 filled them WHERE centroid IS NULL, and pipelines/districts-tiger has not run since. Two consequences the next annual TIGER refresh delivers together: (1) any NEWLY published district inserts with centroid NULL, and the backfill that would have fixed it is a migration that has already run; (2) worse and quieter, every EXISTING district keeps the centroid computed from its OLD polygon while its boundary_geometry is rewritten, so map labels drift off their shapes wherever a boundary moved. Verified on prod 2026-09-10: 7211 tiger district rows, 0 null centroids, so nothing is broken yet — this is a loaded gun, not a wound. Fix is one line in each branch of the RPC, using the project convention ST_PointOnSurface(v_geom)::geometry(Point,4326) (see 20260528170000 and 20260528180100 — NOT ST_Centroid, which can land outside an irregular or multipart polygon). Surfaced by [[FIX-914]], whose derive_nh_floterials() does refresh centroid on its DO UPDATE — so after a tiger_year bump the 39 derived NH floterials would carry correct centroids while the 164 base districts they are unions of carried stale ones. Cross-ref [[FIX-914]].  APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-135, CLOSED, prod): both paths now write ST_PointOnSurface(v_geom)::geometry(Point,4326) -- migration 20260919011000, pushed to Pro 01:39 UTC. The bullet's premise held exactly: prod read before the change was 7,250 district rows / 7,250 centroids / 0 NULL (7,211 tiger + the 39 FIX-914 derived), so nothing was wrong yet and nothing was re-run to fix it. districts-tiger is NOT fired here -- the next annual TIGER refresh is the receipt for the update path, which is the only path the live data can ever exercise. The prod body was read back with pg_get_functiondef first and was byte-identical to 20260428061525 on disk, so the migration is that definition plus two lines; SECURITY DEFINER, SET search_path and the FIX-834 grants restated, post-push ACL {postgres,service_role} unchanged. Covered by packages/data/src/data/district-upsert-centroid.test.ts, which fires the RPC twice at a fixture polygon inside a rolled-back transaction and asserts the centroid MOVES -- the assertion that fails on the old body -- plus a C-shaped polygon whose ST_Centroid falls outside it, pinning the ST_PointOnSurface convention rather than just the presence of a point. <!--id:FIX-1171-->
- 🔴 M — **DROPPED-OUT cannot mean 'retracted' from a single emit run — the FIX-1106 apply would delete a sitting senator's $6.03M** — cc-119 STOPPED the FIX-1106 apply before it ran; this is what it found. THE INSTRUMENT IS SOUND AND THE PREMISE IS NOT. The keyed audit against emit run 84470d3b (cycle 2026, fec_bulk_indiv, complete 2026-09-14T00:23:02Z, 1,014,747 keys) is internally exact: slice 2,718 recipients / 1,107,506 rows / $1,388,174,001, emit set 2,590 recipients, DROPPED-OUT 128 recipients / 67,933 rows / $106,645,325, PARTIAL 415 / 24,826 / $35,643,113, and 2,718-2,590=128 and 1,107,506-1,014,747=92,759=67,933+24,826 to the row. The keyed re-check reported match 128, shrunk 0, grown 0, gone 0. PROBLEM ONE, the harness number cannot describe this slice: FIX-1106 quotes 3,416 recipients / 209,017 rows / $1,481,928,516 as 2,736 financial entities plus 680 officials, measured 2026-08-25 by the bracket-row proxy, but every row in this slice is to_type=official across 2,718 officials, so a residue set cannot have 3,416 recipients out of 2,718, cannot be worth more than the $1,388,174,001 it is a subset of, and cannot be majority-FE in a population with zero FE. The proxy was almost certainly spanning all four FEC sources; the number that needs re-deriving is the bullet's, not the audit's. PROBLEM TWO and it stops the apply on its own: fec_emit_runs contains exactly ONE fec_bulk_indiv run, ever. A single observation cannot separate retraction (FEC used to report this recipient and stopped, so the rows are stale) from a standing match gap (the pipeline has never resolved this recipient, the rows pre-date the instrument, and they are real). The audit calls both DROPPED-OUT because from one run they are identical. The cohort looks like the second: no DROPPED-OUT recipient has a September write at all, 101 of 128 (55,722 rows) were last written in July 2026, and the set includes Susan Collins -- sitting senator, Maine, up for election in 2026, 3,248 rows, $6,033,338, every row written inside one 51-minute window on 2026-07-27 and untouched since, with zero emit keys in any cycle-2026 run of any source. FEC is certainly still reporting individual contributions to her committee. Context: the same run logged FIX-937 excluding 13,189 of 32,989 officials from the name-match pool, so there IS a gate in front of the emit path, and 128 officials receiving zero emitted rows is the shape a gate produces. WHAT MAKES IT DECIDABLE, none of it expensive: (1) a SECOND complete emit run, next due Sun 2026-09-20 -- a recipient present in one complete set and absent from the next is a retraction, absent from both is unproven; (2) re-derive the bullet's figure per-source across indiv_to_committee, pac and ie and sum, which is what the proxy was measuring; (3) ask the narrow question for one recipient -- does the current indiv2026 file contain contributions to Collins's committee, because a yes moves the whole DROPPED-OUT class out of FIX-1106 and into a match-regression FIX. Until at least (1), the apply must not run: it would destroy $106.6M of contribution history on one observation. Manifest and full write-up committed at docs/audits/2026-09-14-fix1106-residue-2026-indiv.md and its .tsv. Cross-ref [[FIX-1106]], [[FIX-937]], [[FIX-1165]], [[FIX-957]]. **EXPERIMENTS (2) AND (3) RAN 2026-09-14 (cc-126) — both answered, and (3) REFUTES THIS BULLET'S OWN EXAMPLE.** (2) the per-source decomposition is on [[FIX-1106]]; the short version is that the proxy overstated recipients 4.5x and dollars 2.3x but was within 4.4% on rows, and fec_bulk_pac adds 134 officials / 20,165 rows / \$70,305,398 that the proxy never covered. (3) THE COLLINS ANSWER, and it is not the one this bullet predicts: **there are TWO Susan Collins rows and both hold S6ME00159.** The \$6,033,338 called 'a sitting senator's' above is on c6825cc2, role_title **Candidate for Senator**; the sitting senator is 3e559d5c, role_title **Senator**, who holds 5,243 cycle-2026 indiv rows / \$8,879,021 — and **ALL 5,243 were emitted by run 84470d3b**. Both role_titles are in FEC_ELECTABLE_ROLE_TITLES and both map to office S, so the [[FIX-937]] gate excludes neither; the matcher simply moved from the candidate row to the elected row, and the 3,248 stale rows are the double-count [[FIX-1106]] exists to remove. Generalising across all 128: **88 have a same-CAND_ID sibling that WAS emitted (46,561 rows / \$72,951,060 — 68.4% of the class's dollars), 95 have such a sibling at all, 1 has no CAND_ID.** Of the 40 with no emitted twin, 5 (2,650 rows / \$3,168,202) fail the [[FIX-1025]] role-office rule by holding a HOUSE id on a Senator row (Roger Marshall H6KS01179, Adam B. Schiff H0CA27085). **VERDICT: match gap / binding artefact, not retraction, for at least 71% of the class.** Filed as [[FIX-1187]]. What experiment (1) still has to decide shrinks to **34 officials / 18,533 rows / \$30,261,581** — role and office agreeing, no emitted twin. The apply stays blocked until Sun 2026-09-20 regardless. Evidence: docs/audits/2026-09-14-fix1182-experiments-2-3.md. **cc-128 (2026-09-16) narrows what experiment 1 is for.** With [[FIX-1187]]'s 151 shared-CAND_ID pairs accounted for, the residue this experiment has to decide is only the twin-less class — roughly 34 officials / 18,533 rows / $30.26M on the indiv arm — not the whole DROPPED-OUT set. The second complete emitting run is still the instrument; it just has far less to rule on, and the residue audit should be re-run after 1187 lands so the class is measured clean. cc-129 note: with [[FIX-1187]] set 1 applied, experiment 1 now decides only the twin-less residue — the 151 shared-CAND_ID twins are merged and no longer present a DROPPED-OUT signature of their own. cc-141 deferred experiment (1) — the three prod residue audits were out of window (the session fell inside the 22:30-01:00 blackout) and Craig deferred them. One premise MOVED underneath the design meanwhile: set A's prod copy is GONE. Run 84470d3b was wiped by the 2026-09-15 crash recovery because fec_emit_runs and fec_emit_keys are UNLOGGED (see [[FIX-1184]]), so a two-SET key comparison is no longer available from the database at all. Set A survives ONLY as the tracked 09-14 manifests under docs/audits/ — indiv carries run_id 84470d3b-d147-5a01-b43a-e4a4d7930890, 1,014,747 keys, DROPPED-OUT 128 recipients / 67,933 rows / $106,645,325 — and that file is now the sole authority the experiment can join against.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, CLOSING as redirected): experiment (1) ran for all three sources against the second complete emit run and is written up in docs/audits/2026-09-22-fix1182-experiment-1.md. THE EXPECTATION THIS BULLET CARRIED WAS INVERTED. It predicted class (ii) RETRACTION for indiv would be the twin-less cohort, ~34 officials / 18,533 rows / $30,261,581; measured, that cohort is class (i) UNPROVEN — 35 / 18,722 / $30,526,063 — i.e. the recipients BOTH complete runs declined to emit, which is exactly the class this FIX was filed to protect. Class (ii) is ONE official for indiv and TWO for pac, 683 rows / $626,233 in total. The hazard is now priced rather than argued: class (iii) says a single-run apply on 09-14 would have deleted 211 recipients / 69,069 rows / $145,156,574 that the very next complete run re-emitted — twenty-four times the one senator's $6.03M this FIX was filed on. Two findings the design did not claim: (1) 'absent from A's DROPPED-OUT list' is not sufficient for retraction, because the rows may not have existed when A was cut, so the classifier demands the rows' own last_write predate set A and demotes anything younger to UNPROVEN — without it a recipient created between the runs would be classified retracted and deleted; (2) that provenance check caught a parse bug on its first run, because Postgres's '+00' zone is not valid ISO and new Date() returns Invalid Date, so every candidate demoted and the experiment reported an empty class (ii) for exactly the right-looking reason — a fail-closed check that fails closed for a parse bug is indistinguishable from a true negative. The retraction class now lives in [[FIX-1106]]'s derived manifests, so this redirects there. <!--id:FIX-1182-->

- 🟠 M — **FEC ITC-arm DROPPED-OUT is a COMMITTEE-side binding question, and 626 committees / $536.8M have no answer** — cc-126 measured the cycle-2026 itemised-committee (ITC) DROPPED-OUT class at 626 committees / $536.8M, and cc-128 confirmed the individual and pac arms of that class are dominated by FIX-1187 shape A (88 of 128 indiv, 117 of 134 pac are shared-CAND_ID twins). The ITC arm is NOT: it is keyed on committees, not officials, so 1187 does not touch it. Collins is the worked example — her three committees are DROPPED-OUT on ITC with zero emitted keys, and her principal committee C00314575 has no financial_entities row at all. Until this is answered the FIX-1106 apply cannot sweep the ITC source, because a committee with no FE row cannot be distinguished from a committee whose binding was retracted. Needs its own census first: for the 626, how many have an FE row, how many resolve to an official, and how many are simply unminted. **CENSUS DELIVERED 2026-09-20 (cc-139) — `docs/audits/2026-09-20-fix1188-itc-committee-census.md`. The four counts, and the finding that the question had the wrong shape.** Read-only on prod against the 626 manifest ids. **(a) 626/626 have a `financial_entities` row**, and 626/626 carry a non-NULL `fec_committee_id`. **(b) 0/626 resolve to an official.** **(c) 0/626 are unminted — and unminted is IMPOSSIBLE in this manifest by construction**, because its key column is `to_id` with `to_type='financial_entity'` on all 808 rows, so the key IS an FE id and a committee with no FE row cannot appear in the file. This bullet's stated blocker — 'a committee with no FE row cannot be distinguished from a committee whose binding was retracted' — therefore does not describe this manifest. **(d) Collins:** C00314575 confirmed to have NO `financial_entities` row on prod AND to be absent from the manifest; as a P-designation committee it routes ROUTE_CAND to her official row (`3e559d5c…`, `fec_candidate_id` S6ME00159), so 'no FE row' is the designed state for it rather than a defect. Her three committees that ARE among the 626 are COLLINS VICTORY (C00692897, 694 rows / $2,912,054), COLLINS VICTORY FUND (C00824151, 420 / $1,456,227) and **SUSAN COLLINS FOR MAINE (C00920389, 16 / $338,500) — a sitting senator's own 2026-cycle campaign committee**, stored as `fec_cmte_type_raw='N'` and indistinguishable in the schema from GROW THE MAJORITY. **(b)=0 IS A SCHEMA FACT, NOT A MONEY FACT:** no committee-to-official linkage is persisted anywhere — `officials.source_ids` has no `fec_committee_id` key across 19,600 `fec_candidate_id` holders, there is no `fec_committee_candidate` table, and the CMTE_ID-to-CAND_ID map is built in memory per run by `parseCcl()` and never written. The zero is guaranteed by construction and cannot license a sweep; twelve of the top twenty by dollar are named after sitting officials. **MONDAY RECOMMENDATION: the [[FIX-1106]] apply must EXCLUDE the `fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee` source**, and the missing step is a committee-to-candidate BINDING (persist the ccl P/A linkage and CMTE_DSGN), not a minting pass. Filed as [[FIX-1203]]. This bullet stays OPEN until that binding lands. <!--id:FIX-1188-->
- 🔴 S — **promote_candidate_to_elected DESTROYS the promoted candidate's fec_candidate_id — three CAND_IDs erased from prod on 2026-09-17** — The RPC merges identities as (c.source_ids || e.source_ids) and jsonb || keeps the RIGHT operand on key conflict, so the ELECTED row's fec_candidate_id overwrites the CANDIDATE's — the exact opposite of the comment three lines above it, which says 'candidate's fec_candidate_id is preserved, elected's congress_gov is appended'. The promoted row survives, the elected row is DELETED, and the candidate's own CAND_ID is gone with no trace: not live, not in merged_fec_candidate_ids, not in prior_fec_candidate_ids. Measured on prod 2026-09-17 23:19-23:20 UTC, three promotions fired in one nightly and erased H6NC09200 (Mark Harris), H2NJ13075 (Robert Menendez) and S0KS00315 (Roger Marshall) — all three read 0/0/0 holders across every binding surface, and the local pre-promotion clone still names each one, which is the only reason they are recoverable at all. Marshall is the expensive case: the promoted row was his SENATE stub, so the sitting Senator now carries a HOUSE id and his Senate CAND_ID is unclaimed. Fix is one swap to (e.source_ids || c.source_ids), plus appending the losing id to merged_fec_candidate_ids rather than dropping it; the three erased ids need restoring from the clone. See [[FIX-1187]] and the cc-130 report. CLOSED by cc-131 (2026-09-18). Migration 20260919000000 on Pro at 01:47:37 UTC swaps the operands to (e.source_ids || c.source_ids) and files the losing id into prior_fec_candidate_ids rather than dropping it, unioning the elected row's own prior array in for the same reason; signature unchanged so the FIX-834 grant matrix carries over, no SET clause so check:proconfig stays clean. Four clone cases green plus three source anchors that run with no database. The three erased ids were restored in one keyed transaction at 02:42:5x UTC via the new data:fec:restore-ids:prod, holder census 0/0/0 to 1/1/1, and an authoritative-claim census over all six ids returns exactly one holder each: H6NC09200 and H4NC08066 to Mark Harris, H2NJ13075 and H2NJ08232 to Robert Menendez, S0KS00315 and H6KS01179 to Roger Marshall. Two findings worth carrying. First, cc-131's design would have re-created the defect for Harris and Menendez: it filed a same-chamber loser into merged_fec_candidate_ids, and authoritativeClaims() FILTERS retired ids out, so those two would have gone into Sunday's drop with zero authoritative holders and been re-minted exactly as if never restored - merged is not a weaker claim, it is the absence of one, and only fec_candidate_id and prior_fec_candidate_ids are read by buildMatchIndex pass 1 and loadOfficialsByFecIds. All three went to prior instead and the migration follows the same rule. Second, the script's own post-write check was wrong and was fixed in the same run: it counted a retired marker on ANY row as a holder, so a correct restore exited 2 reporting 2 holders for the previously-live ids, when those markers are set 1's merge stubs recording the ids they gave up - the correct post-merge shape. Evidence in docs/audits/2026-09-18-fix1195-restore.md. <!--id:FIX-1195-->
- 🔴 M — **A shared-CAND_ID merge UNBLOCKS the FIX-248 promotion's ambiguity guard, and the next nightly deletes the merge's own survivor** — promote-candidates skips a pair unless exactly one candidate row matches (normName(full_name), state, role_family) — 'if (matches.length !== 1) continue'. An official carrying TWO candidate stubs is therefore permanently skipped. Retiring one stub's fec_candidate_id is exactly what merge-same-person-official-dupes does, and the candidate load filters on fec_candidate_id NOT NULL, so the merge drops that stub out of the index and collapses 2 matches to 1. The promotion then fires on the FIRST nightly afterwards, promotes the surviving stub, and DELETES the merge's survivor row. Measured: FIX-1187 set 1 landed 2026-09-17 00:23-03:53 UTC; the 23:18 nightly promoted three of its 151 survivors out of existence (Mark Harris, Robert Menendez, Roger Marshall — the only three whose elected and candidate full_name strings match exactly, the rest differing by a middle initial). Consequences: any manifest naming a survivor UUID is invalidated silently, which is what killed cc-130's set 2; and each promotion erases a CAND_ID via [[FIX-1195]]. Bounded this time — of the 148 surviving set-1 survivors, 147 now have 0 candidate matches and 1 has 2, so NONE sit at exactly 1 and no further promotions are queued from that cohort. Fixes to weigh: have the merge leave the stub visible to the promotion's filter, or teach promote-candidates to skip a stub whose CAND_ID was retired by a merge, or run the promotion before remediation rather than after. See [[FIX-1187]] and the cc-130 report. CLOSED by cc-131 (2026-09-18, on origin/main at 01:45 UTC). Guard shape (a): steps 3 and 4 of promote-candidates move into an exported pure selectPromotionPairs(), and an elected row holding source_ids.fec_candidate_id is dropped BEFORE indexing, so it never reaches the ambiguity test at all; skippedBound joins PromoteCandidatesResult and prints as skipped_bound=N. Seven structural cases, the load-bearing pair differing only in that one key. Measured: on the clone (pre-set-1 state) 2 pairs detected before the guard and 0 after, both would-have-deleted rows being FEC-bound, with 524 elected rows carrying a binding; on prod 0 before and 0 after with 527 bound, because the promotion has already consumed all three. Prod's zero is not evidence the guard is idle - set 2 changes every survivor's candidate-match count again, which is why this landed before the restore and before set 2. The nightly's own line puts the mechanism beyond inference: detected=3 promoted=3 failed=0 at 2026-09-17T23:20:46Z (GHA 35286154536), with Harris 668 votes + 2,763 FKs, Menendez 1,593 + 11,916, Marshall 1,879 + 3,097. That run was on unguarded main; the first guarded nightly fires ~21:00 UTC 2026-09-18 and its skipped_bound line is the prod evidence, which cc-132 reads. APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-132, PROD EVIDENCE — and a correction to how many guarded nightlies exist): the guard is live and measured. The 2026-09-19 nightly (GHA run 35403626528, fec-phase, 2026-09-18T22:57:37Z) logged 'promote-candidates: detected=0 promoted=0 failed=0 skipped_bound=527' — 527 pairs refused because the elected row already holds a fec_candidate_id, and detected=0/promoted=0 means it touched nothing, which is why cc-131's three restored CAND_IDs (H6NC09200, H2NJ13075, S0KS00315) were still 1/1/1 when cc-132 re-read them at 18:0x UTC the next day and still 1/1/1 after set 2. CORRECTION: only ONE guarded nightly has run, not two. The 2026-09-18 nightly's promotion executed at 2026-09-17T23:20:46Z and emits no skipped_bound field at all — 'detected=3 promoted=3 failed=0' — because this FIX landed at 2026-09-18T01:30:41Z (sha 009b0600), 2 h 10 m LATER. That unguarded 23:20:46 run is the one the FIX-1187 v2 manifest header names as having deleted Marshall's survivor row 27090d3a, the damage cc-131's FIX-1195 restore then repaired. So the two nightlies are not two receipts for the guard: one is the receipt, the other is the injury. Anything asking for 'both guarded nightlies' should read the 09-19 run and then wait for 09-20. Second receipt (cc-141; drop night 2026-09-20 22:49 to 09-21 00:21 UTC, GHA run 35542803868). promote-candidates: detected=0 promoted=0 failed=0 skipped_bound=527, with no 'moved' line; cn26: lines=8620 new=3 existing=8616 name_updated=19 no_office=0 failed=0; stubs for the twelve held ids = 0; and the run's own minting = 3 rows at 22:53:36.428 UTC, equal to cn26's new=3 (two instruments, one population). All twelve ids resolve to exactly ONE authoritative holder each, every one [elected], the House id as prior and the Senate id as live on a single row per person: Schiff, Peters, Curtis, Lummis, Marshall, Menendez, Harris. NOTE that the cc-141 prompt's stated expectations were wrong in two places and the DATA is right: set 1 is not four live ids (only its one Senate id, S0KS00315, is live), and merged=1 on nine of the twelve is the CORRECT post-merge shape per restore-fec-ids.ts:105-108, not a failure. <!--id:FIX-1196-->
- 🟠 M — **The 626 ITC DROPPED-OUT committees / $536.8M cannot be swept: no committee-to-candidate binding is persisted anywhere, and a sitting senator's own campaign committee sits in the class** — Census filed at docs/audits/2026-09-20-fix1188-itc-committee-census.md (cc-139, read-only on prod 2026-09-20 19:22-19:55 UTC), answering [[FIX-1188]]. THE COUNTS: of the 626 ITC DROPPED-OUT committees, 626/626 have a financial_entities row AND a non-NULL fec_committee_id; 0/626 resolve to an official; 0/626 are unminted. The unminted class is impossible by construction — the manifest's key column is to_id with to_type=financial_entity for all 808 rows, so the key IS an FE id and a committee with no FE row cannot appear. FIX-1188's stated blocker ('a committee with no FE row cannot be distinguished from a committee whose binding was retracted') therefore does not describe this manifest. WHY (b)=0 IS A SCHEMA FACT, NOT A MONEY FACT: no persisted committee-to-official linkage exists anywhere. officials.source_ids carries fec_candidate_id (19,600 officials) and never a committee id; there is no fec_committee_candidate table; cross-checking all 626 committee ids against officials.source_ids::text returns 0. The CMTE_ID to CAND_ID map is built per-run in memory by parseCcl() from the FEC ccl file as a P/A allow-list and is never written to the DB. THE HAZARD: all 626 are entity_type=pac, none synthetic, FEC type N (621) / Q (4) / V (1) — not one is P or A, which is ROUTE_CMTE working as designed. But twelve of the top twenty by dollar are named after sitting officials (CORNYN, JEFFRIES, OSSOFF, JOHNSON, HUSTED, TORRES, BOOKER, MORENO, MOODY, EMMER, COOPER, WHATLEY), and SUSAN COLLINS FOR MAINE (C00920389, type N, $338,500 over 16 rows) is a sitting senator's own 2026-cycle campaign committee sitting in the DROPPED-OUT class with nothing in the schema to distinguish it from GROW THE MAJORITY. That is [[FIX-1182]]'s hazard reaching the ITC arm by a second route — [[FIX-1187]] shape A genuinely does not touch ITC, but shape A was never the only way to delete a sitting member's money. SECOND GAP: indiv.ts excludes joint-fundraising committees at source on CMTE_DSGN='J', but we store only CMTE_TP (fec_cmte_type_raw) and not CMTE_DSGN, so prod cannot tell a JFC from a plain PAC — and TRUMP NATIONAL COMMITTEE JFC is row 6 of this class with nine more victory funds in the top twenty. FIX SHAPE: persist the ccl P/A linkage and CMTE_DSGN as a committee-to-candidate binding table so DROPPED-OUT can be told apart from 'a candidate committee we never bound'. This is a binding step, NOT a minting step — there is nothing to mint. Until it exists the FIX-1106 apply must EXCLUDE the fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee source; FIX-1188's sweep-if-empty condition must not be honoured on a (b)=0 that the schema guarantees. Cross-ref [[FIX-1106]] [[FIX-1182]] [[FIX-1187]] [[FIX-1188]] [[FIX-701]] [[FIX-236]]. cc-141 did NOT measure the ITC classes — the three residue audits were out of window and deferred, so the experiment-(1) classification for fec_bulk_indiv_to_committee is still owed. Unchanged: ITC remains EXCLUDED from any [[FIX-1106]] apply.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, READ ONLY — measured support, STAYS OPEN): the 09-22 re-cut returns ITC DROPPED-OUT 626 recipients / 106,278 rows / $536,845,833 — every figure IDENTICAL to 2026-09-14, across two complete emit runs eight days apart. [[FIX-1182]] experiment (1) puts all 626 in class (i) UNPROVEN and makes classes (ii) RETRACTION and (iii) RE-EMITTED both exactly zero. That is not 'stable residue': a residue set the source is still writing shrinks (indiv went 128 -> 36, pac 134 -> 18 over the same window), and a set that does not move by a single row is one nothing in the emit path ever reaches. So this bullet's claim — that no committee-to-candidate binding is persisted anywhere — now has an independent measurement behind it rather than only an argument, and the 626 are structurally unemitted rather than decaying residue awaiting a sweep. No manifest was cut for ITC and its apply is EMPTY by construction.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, post-apply): ITC was excluded from both applies as designed and no ITC manifest exists to apply. The indiv and pac applies deleted 683 rows between them; ITC's 626 recipients / 106,278 rows / $536,845,833 are untouched and remain UNPROVEN on the two-run evidence. <!--id:FIX-1203-->

## DB PERFORMANCE / COMPUTE
- 🟡 S — **Skip query_districts geometry RPC for empty-leaf /districts/[id] (FIX-683 item 4 deferral)** — Deferred from [[FIX-683]]. FIX-683 shipped the heavy-RPC skip for empty /jurisdictions/[id] (cache-miss → base-row shell, no boundary read) and empty /officials/[id] (skip get_official_page), but NOT for /districts/[id]. The district page's heavy read is query_districts (the simplified-geometry PostGIS RPC — the 492MB geometry), and it runs in BOTH loadDistrict (page) AND generateMetadata. For an empty-leaf district (not in jurisdiction_page_cache — FIX-683 already noindexes these via lookupJurisdictionCache), skipping the geometry RPC and rendering a light 'no boundary data yet' shell would remove the last cold-read class. NOT done in FIX-683 because: (a) the boundary map is the district page's primary content even for districts with no cross-linked officials (the OpenStates name-match gap), so a product call is needed on whether empty-by-our-cache districts should still show their boundary; (b) generateMetadata currently calls loadDistrict (geometry + officials + parent) just to build a title — that should be slimmed to a cheap name fetch regardless. Scope: gate loadDistrict's query_districts call on lookupJurisdictionCache membership (fail-open), and replace district generateMetadata's loadDistrict call with a name-only fetch + the existing membership lookup. Verify: empty district renders shell + no query_districts in pg_stat_statements; content district unchanged. <!--id:FIX-684-->
- 🟡 M — **Senate roll-call XML carries lis_member_id — resolve Senators by stable id instead of lastname:state** — **File only — the gate result from [[FIX-940]]. Do NOT attempt the crosswalk ingest as part of that PR.** Senate LIS roll-call XML carries a **stable per-member identifier the writer has never read**. Verified 2026-07-31 against two live 119th-Congress rolls (`vote_119_1_00100.xml`, `vote_119_2_00010.xml`): every `<member>` block has exactly seven fields — `member_full`, `last_name`, `first_name`, `party`, `state`, `vote_cast`, and **`lis_member_id`** (e.g. `S428` for Alsobrooks, `S409` for Luján). `packages/data/src/pipelines/congress/votes.ts` reads only `last_name`, `state` and `vote_cast`, which is why the Senate branch fell back to a name key at all — the House branch resolves by bioguide from `@_name-id` and is correct precisely because it has a stable id. [[FIX-940]] fixed the name path (tier filter + collision refusal + diacritic folding) but left it the PRIMARY key; this makes it the FALLBACK. **What it needs: a crosswalk.** `grep` confirms the repo stores no LIS id anywhere today — not on `officials.source_ids`, not in any migration. The `unitedstates/congress-legislators` dataset carries `id.lis` alongside `id.bioguide`, so the work is: ingest that mapping, persist it as `officials.source_ids->>'lis_member_id'` (same JSONB convention as `congress_gov`/`fec_id`), then key the Senate map on it with the name map retained for members the crosswalk misses. **Why it is worth doing even though [[FIX-940]] closed the live bug:** the name key is intrinsically ambiguous — two sitting Senators sharing `(surname, state)` is legal and [[FIX-940]]'s guard deliberately REFUSES that slot rather than guessing, which turns the ambiguity into dropped votes instead of misfiled ones. An id-keyed map has no such failure mode. It also removes the diacritic dependency [[FIX-940]] had to add: the XML spells every surname ASCII-only while `officials` stores "Luján", and he was the ONLY sitting Senator affected — a fragile one-of-one that a stable id makes moot. **Cheaper intermediate if the crosswalk is deferred:** the XML also carries `first_name`, which the writer ignores today; folding it into the key as a tie-breaker would disambiguate most same-surname/same-state collisions with no new data source, though it is still name-matching and inherits FEC-legal-name skew (Ted/Rafael Cruz, Jack/John Reed both appear in this exact dataset). Cross-ref [[FIX-940]], [[FIX-545]], [[FIX-837]]. <!--id:FIX-948-->
- 🟠 M — **entity-connection-stats-rebuild sits at 86% of the 6h ceiling with no budget guard, no cursor and no partial status — bimodal 101s..18,568s, never failed, so FIX-969 cannot see it** — **Found by the 2026-08-07 site-wide efficiency audit (`docs/audits/2026-08-07-efficiency-audit.md`); three independent enumeration passes converged on it, and it SURVIVED adversarial verification at 🟠** — the cron-envelope pass (class 4), the live-plpgsql-loop pass (class 1) and the tuned-constant pass (class 3). **It is NOT one of [[FIX-969]]'s seven**, because FIX-969 enumerated by *failure* and this job has **0 failures in 8 retained runs** — it is the job most likely to join that list next, and the clearest instance of the audit's whole premise. **Measured, `cron.job_run_details` by jobname (playbook D3), full retained window:** p95 **17,296 s = 80.1%** of the 21,600 s ceiling; max **18,568 s = 86.0%**; headroom **3,032 s (50.5 min)**. Trail for `0 11 * * 1,3`: 07-13 Mon 7,112 s · 07-15 Wed 136 s · 07-20 Mon 128 s · 07-22 Wed 132 s · 07-27 Mon **18,568 s** · 07-29 Wed 7,095 s · 08-03 Mon **14,933 s** · 08-05 Wed 101 s. **This is bimodal, not trending** — 101–136 s or 7,095–18,568 s, a **184× spread** with no rising curve; Monday mean 10,185 s vs Wednesday mean 1,866 s (**5.5×**). It fires at 11:00, three hours after the `rebuild-ec-incremental*` jobs at 08:00 that FIX-969 shows dying at ~14:00 on the ceiling — so on a blowout day it runs *concurrently with a six-hour squatter*. Note this is a **distinct mechanism from FIX-969's**: those jobs are starved *before they start* (the ~10 s libpq window → `job startup timeout`); this one **starts fine and runs 5.5× slower**. Contention, not starvation. **The structural defect:** the live body of `public.rebuild_entity_connection_stats` (migration `20260710000000_fix717_718_incremental_stats_rollup_tables.sql:477 c_bounds[16]` / `:555 FOR i IN 1..16`) has **no predictive budget guard, no reservation, no chunk cursor and no `partial` status** — verified against the live body: `has_budget=false`, `has_cursor=false`. Its 16-window quantum governs only the cheap *apply* phase; the dominant cost, the **stage build**, is a **single unchunked statement** aggregating `2 × 5,059,971 = 10,119,942` rows (UNION ALL over `entity_connections`). So the 6 h `statement_timeout` is its **only stop**, and per playbook C2 that timer **arms once at CALL start** and is not re-armed by per-chunk `COMMIT`s. When it fires, the entire stage build is discarded and the next run restarts at window 1 — the exact shape [[FIX-944]] and [[FIX-965]] were built to eliminate elsewhere. **Consequence if it crosses:** `entity_connection_stats_mv` is a 2,418,981-row / 470 MB table read on the request path ([[FIX-774]]); a silent 6 h blowout leaves it stale with nothing watching — `check_cron_job_health()` reports blowouts as REPORT-ONLY by design, and the freshness canary's `ROLLUP_PIPELINES` does not enumerate it. **Fix shape:** apply the playbook A4 chunked-writer skeleton — chunk the **stage build** by `to_id`/`from_id` RANGE aligned to the read index (A2, not an entity list), advance a cursor inside each chunk transaction, add the predictive `elapsed + slowest × 1.25 > budget` stop with a session-scoped GUC override and a `partial` status, and abort-not-skip on chunk failure. Take the [[FIX-974]] `donor_rollup_rebuild_bulk` body as the reference implementation rather than re-deriving it. Separately worth deciding: whether the 11:00 Mon/Wed slot should move off the tail of the 08:00 EC rebuild window at all. **Cross-cutting note:** `refresh_contract_flow_rollups` has the same shape (single un-COMMITted transaction, no chunking, no budget) and grew **7.2× in one week** — filed separately. Cross-ref [[FIX-969]], [[FIX-944]], [[FIX-965]], [[FIX-974]], [[FIX-717]], [[FIX-718]], [[FIX-774]], [[FIX-968]]. **CLOSED 2026-09-20 (cc-139) — recognized; the fix shape this bullet specified was built by [[FIX-1111]]/[[FIX-1161]] as a crawl arm rather than as a chunked writer.** Verified read-only on prod, all three legs: (1) `cron.job` jobid 16 `entity-connection-stats-rebuild` is **`active=f`**; (2) `rollup_watch_overrides` carries `pipeline='entity_connection_stats_rebuild'` with **`retired_at = 2026-08-26 16:00:00+00`** and the note 'FIX-1161. Retired: the entity-connection stats rebuild moved into the bounded ec-crawl arm'; (3) **the arm is LIVE and bounded** — `data_sync_log` records **407** `entity_connection_stats_windows` units between 2026-08-28 08:45 and 2026-09-20 19:15, one bankable unit per `ec-crawl` firing under `run_entity_connections_rebuild`. That delivers this bullet's ask (chunk the stage build, carry a cursor, expose a `partial` status, bound the run) by replacing the single 16-window CALL rather than chunking inside it. The job's own last two firings (08-24, 08-26) both died `server restarted` before it was parked, and it has not fired since. **DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED:** `entity-connection-stats-rebuild` still carries a 9,000 s `cron_job_budget` row, and `rebuild-ec-incremental` / `rebuild-ec-incremental-mon` still carry **18,000 s** rows — above the 3 h (10,800 s) `postgres` ceiling — all three on `active=f` jobs; Craig's 'revisit before re-enable' stands and cc-139 left them alone. Residual lives in [[FIX-1111]] and the budget-ledger item. <!--id:FIX-982-->
