- 🟠 M — **A bulk landing must PRE-DRAIN the derived-layer debt it creates, in the same quiet window — not leave it for the next scheduled firing** — STANDING CONVENTION, proposed. The 2026-08-19 saturation is the cleanest instance yet of a repeating shape: a bulk write lands cheaply and hands an unbounded bill to whatever derives from it. The FEC same-person merge and re-split work bulk-rewrote financial_relationships; that rewrite bumped updated_at on ~70 percent of all donors; the EC incremental donations arm is watermark-driven, so its dirty set went from a normal few thousand to 3,357,701 rows over 1,961,194 from_ids; and the next scheduled firing spent six hours on it, wrote zero, and took the request path down with it. NONE of that cost appeared in the landing script. It appeared two days later, in a different job, at 08:00 UTC on a weekday, with no operator watching. THE RULE: a script that bulk-rewrites a table owns the derived-layer work its rewrite creates, and drains it in the same quiet window, before it exits. This is the exact sibling of the vacuum rule already standing in CLAUDE.md — any script that bulk-rewrites a table ends by vacuuming what it rewrote (FIX-943) — and it comes from the same failure: the cost is real, it is created by the landing, and leaving it to a background mechanism means it lands on a schedule nobody chose. Vacuum debt and derived-debt are the same category of unpaid bill; the vacuum half is already a written convention and the derived half is not. WHAT PRE-DRAINING MEANS CONCRETELY: after the bulk write and its vacuum, the script drives the derived arms its rewrite dirtied to convergence — for a financial_relationships rewrite that is the EC donations arm and the donor rollup — rather than assuming the weekly cron will absorb it. Post-[[FIX-1069]] this is cheap to do safely, because the donations arm is now 16 committed, budget-checked, resumable windows that can be driven to convergence and re-entered if interrupted. AMENDS THE PR 3b DISPATCH RECIPE: 3b lands per-cycle FEC replays, which is precisely a repeated bulk rewrite of financial_relationships. Its per-cycle dispatch recipe should gain a post-cycle derived-drain step so cycle N pays its own derived cost before cycle N+1 starts, instead of accumulating debt across cycles for a Monday firing to discover. The 3b sessions consume this bullet; it is NOT a request to rebuild the [[FIX-974]] bulk-regime pass here. OPEN QUESTION for whoever implements: whether the drain belongs in each landing script or in one shared post-bulk-landing helper that takes the set of tables rewritten and maps them to the derived arms that consume them. The second is better if more than two landing scripts need it, and the mapping table is small today: financial_relationships to EC donations plus donor rollup plus FE totals. Cross-ref [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-1069]], [[FIX-1018]], [[FIX-933]], [[FIX-974]], [[FIX-1002]]. APPEND 2026-09-09 (THE OPEN QUESTION IS ANSWERED, AND THE HELPER EXISTS). This bullet asked 'whether the drain belongs in each landing script or in one shared post-bulk-landing helper... The second is better if more than two landing scripts need it.' The tree answers it rather than taste: THREE scripts already carried a byte-for-byte identical manifest-scoped tail -- remediate-role-ineligible-holders, remediate-cross-person-misattribution, merge-same-person-official-dupes -- and FIX-1106's residue sweep is a fourth. New module packages/data/src/lib/fr-rewrite-drain.ts holds it: entity_connections money-edge delete keyed on the deleted FR row ids (the 954:693 shape, scoped by evidence_source), donor_rollup_rebuild_recipients(affected), then financial_entity_donation_totals_rebuild and donor_party_rollup_rebuild_donors chunked at 5,000 donors. All four scripts now call it and none inlines those steps any more. TWO DESIGN POINTS WORTH THE INK. (1) The affected-officials table name AND its id column are parameters, because the three callers genuinely disagree -- _affected(official_id), _affected(id), _affected_officials(id) -- and an assumption there would have been a silent wrong-column bug. (2) THE HELPER'S SCOPE STOPS AT THE MANIFEST'S COST. It executes no platform-scoped step, with or without --defer-tails, and issues no VACUUM; those stay in the callers that already implement them correctly. That is FIX-1165's rule expressed as a boundary rather than a flag, and it also protects the FIX-965 hazard by construction: refresh_treemap_individuals_global() must never run under an armed statement_timeout, and folding it into a generic step loop in a shared helper is exactly how that would have come back. A source-reading unit test fails if any rebuild_/refresh_ function other than the three manifest-scoped ones appears in the helper, with a floor assertion so it cannot pass vacuously. THE BULK-LANDING HALF IS ALREADY THIS BULLET'S OWN and is NOT built here -- 'AMENDS THE PR 3b DISPATCH RECIPE... its per-cycle dispatch recipe should gain a post-cycle derived-drain step'. No new FIX was allocated for it; when 3b next lands a per-cycle replay, drainFrRewrite is the entry point, and the arm that needs adding there is the EC donations arm driven to convergence (cheap post-FIX-1069, 16 committed budget-checked windows). Bullet stays OPEN on that half. APPEND 2026-09-14 (cc-119, STILL OPEN, AND A SCHEDULED LANDING JUST DEMONSTRATED IT). cc-119 would have exercised the helper half via the FIX-1106 residue apply; that apply was STOPPED before it ran (its manifest contradicted its own premise -- see docs/audits/2026-09-14-fix1106-residue-2026-indiv.md), so the helper half gained no new receipt and the bulk-landing half is untouched. What DID happen is a clean measurement of this bullet's thesis from a SCHEDULED landing rather than a supervised one. The 2026-09-13 nightly ran the season's first emitting FEC pass, 3,681,379 rows committed at 00:23:45Z. Every ec-crawl and fe-crawl firing from 22:30 to 00:00 took 0.07-0.35 s. The four firings after the commit took 176.3 s (ec 00:30), 230.3 s (fe 00:30), 301.8 s (ec 00:45) and 329.3 s (ec 01:00), with fe 01:00 still running past 430 s -- a 500-4700x step change that the landing created and none of which appears in the landing's own runtime. fr-vacuum-analyze went 11.6 s (09-07) to 200.7 s (09-14) for the same reason. So the derived bill is real, it is large, and on the nightly path there is no script to attach a pre-drain to -- which means the fix shape for the scheduled case is not the same as for a supervised landing, and this bullet has only ever specified the supervised one. That gap is the thing to design next. cc-141 did NOT exercise the convention: item 7's apply never ran (out of window), so the drain-and-wait block in docs/cc/PROMPT_TEMPLATE.md remains un-exercised by a second prod op and the closes-as-recognized condition is not met.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, STAYS OPEN — NOT exercised): cc-144 was the run scheduled to exercise the pre-drain convention and it did not get there. Gates (f) and (g) failed at 04:54 UTC and the [[FIX-1106]] apply is stopped at Craig's per-set go-ahead, so no landing ran and no derived debt was created to drain. The convention itself is carried in docs/cc/PROMPT_TEMPLATE.md and was restated in this run's prompt; what is still missing is a landing that actually runs under it. Note that the landing now queued is tiny — 683 rows across 2 officials — so even when it runs it will be weak evidence for a convention written for bulk rewrites; the closing instance should be a landing whose derived debt is measurable.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, EXERCISED — superseding this bullet's earlier cc-144 append, which was written before the apply ran): the landing did run under the block, and the convention held only because the run repaired its own machinery mid-flight. Read the closure narrowly. The pre-drain step this FIX exists to mandate was BROKEN — fr-rewrite-drain.ts step 1 had never parsed against the schema ([[FIX-1210]]) — so the indiv landing committed its DELETE and then threw, which is precisely the failure mode this bullet describes, arriving through a code defect rather than an omission. It was repaired inside the same quiet window rather than left for the next scheduled firing, which is the convention being honoured; but nothing here demonstrates that the machinery is sound, because its only successful prod execution to date is the 8-row pac landing that followed the fix. The landing was also 683 rows, too small to exercise a convention written for bulk rewrites. So this closes as recognized on the strength of the convention being carried and followed, NOT on the strength of the tooling being proven — the next bulk landing is still the real test. <!--id:FIX-1074-->
- 🟡 M — **Every remaining EC arm is still ONE unbudgetable statement — extend FIX-1069's windowing, but drive it off measured arm_timings, not intuition** — STRUCTURAL FOLLOW-UP to [[FIX-1069]], deliberately filed rather than built. FIX-1069 windowed the donations arm because it was the one measurably at the ceiling. The other ten arms — votes, cosponsors, appointments, oversight, holds, gifts, contracts, lobbying, external, investigation — still run through the generic loop as a SINGLE top-level statement each: EXECUTE format SELECT COALESCE(SUM(edges_upserted), 0) FROM public.arm_fn(). So every one of them carries the same structural defect the donations arm did: the budget is checked BEFORE an arm and cannot stop one already running, banking happens AFTER an arm returns, and a statement-timeout cancel rolls the whole arm back. An arm that outgrows the budget produces zero and banks nothing, exactly as 2026-08-19 did. THE CONTRACTS ARM SPECIFICALLY — the honest answer is NOT YET. It is the obvious suspect because the 08-17 run died naming it, but [[FIX-1056]] established that is where the axe FELL, not where the time WENT: the donations arm had already burned 11,824s of that run. Measured on prod on an idle box, the contracts aggregate is 128 SECONDS (GroupAggregate 128,408ms over a Bitmap Heap Scan of 3,908,956 rows), not hours. Windowing it today would be a bad trade, because windowing is not free: the FIX-1069 equivalence run measured a roughly 2x total wall-clock premium (348.6s single-pass vs about 690s across 16 committed windows for identical output). Paying 2x on a 128s arm to make it resumable buys nothing, and the arm is now bounded from outside by [[FIX-1071]] regardless, so it can no longer squat for six hours. TWO REAL RISK SIGNALS ON CONTRACTS, both from FIX-1056's measurement, that make this a WHEN and not an IF: (1) its cost scales with INPUT while its output barely moves — contract+grant input went 1,467,389 to 3,822,744 rows across two July USASpending ingests while output stayed near 189,949 edges, so an edges-per-second metric is structurally blind to its growth; (2) it spills, sorting 359MB external merge against a 256MB work_mem. A further input doubling is an ordinary USASpending event, not a tail case. THE TRIGGER, which is now observable for the first time: FIX-1056 added metadata.arm_timings to every entity_connections_rebuild row, so per-arm cost is finally in the log rather than inferred from watermark side effects. Window any arm whose arm_timings entry crosses roughly 1800s (about 14x contracts' measured cost, and about 10 percent of the 18000s budget) on two consecutive firings. Below that the 2x premium costs more than it saves. PREREQUISITE: several arms would need a natural partition key first. Donations windowed cleanly because from_id is a uuid that partitions evenly into 16 nibble ranges (verified: 175,655 to 177,213 from_ids per window on the full clone). Contracts aggregates on the same from_id, so it inherits that partition directly and is the cheapest one to convert when the trigger fires. Arms keyed on proposals or votes need their own key chosen before this pattern applies to them. Cross-ref [[FIX-1069]], [[FIX-1056]], [[FIX-1071]], [[FIX-703]], [[FIX-1028]]. <!--id:FIX-1075-->
- 🟠 M — **Census the rows=200 planner-fallback signature beyond _conn — GROUP BY over UNION ALL / derived columns gets no statistics** — Census filing in the [[FIX-1112]] shape, not a build. When Postgres groups over an expression it has no statistics for — most commonly a GROUP BY sitting on top of a UNION ALL, or on a derived/computed column rather than a plain table column — it cannot estimate the group count and falls back to a HARDCODED default estimate of 200 rows. That estimate is almost never right, and being wrong by orders of magnitude in the CHEAP direction is what makes it dangerous: a 200-row estimate invites the planner to pick a nested loop, a hash aggregate sized for 200 groups, or a non-parallel path, and then the executor discovers millions of rows at runtime with the wrong plan already committed. The signature is visible in EXPLAIN as a node whose estimate is exactly rows=200 while actual rows is enormous. This was found and fixed in one place (_conn); the point of this item is that NOTHING established it was the only place. THE WORK: grep the whole SQL surface — supabase/migrations, packages/db, and any RPC body in pg_proc.prosrc — for the structural pattern (GROUP BY whose key is a UNION ALL output column or an expression), then EXPLAIN each candidate against PROD, not local, and keep the ones showing rows=200 against a large actual. Prod is the only valid measurement environment here for two reasons: local is cache-warm and small, and per [[reference_clone_understates_scan_bound_costs]] a clone can be ~6x cheap on scan-bound work, so a local EXPLAIN will happily hide the problem. Remedies once a site is confirmed, in increasing order of cost: CREATE STATISTICS on the expression, materialize the derived column so it carries real stats (the [[FIX-1034]] precedent for FR.from_id n_distinct and FE expression stats), or restructure the query so the GROUP BY lands on a plain indexed column. DELIVERABLE is the census table (site, estimate, actual, chosen remedy or explicit no-op), not a set of fixes — sizing the class comes first. Cross-ref [[FIX-1034]] [[FIX-1112]]. <!--id:FIX-1131-->
- 🟡 S — **Measured look: entity_connection_stats_mv.connection_count is definitionally _conn.c — reading the MV would delete the aggregation but couple correctness to a gated arm** — MEASURED LOOK, not a build — the deliverable is a numbers-backed recommendation, and NO-GO is an acceptable and possibly the correct outcome. Observation: entity_connection_stats_mv.connection_count is by definition the same quantity the request path recomputes as _conn.c, so a read path that selected the MV column instead would delete an aggregation from the hot path entirely rather than merely making it cheaper. That is the most attractive shape of optimization available here, which is exactly why it needs measuring before building. THE CATCH, and the reason this is filed as a look: correctness would become coupled to the completion state of a GATED pipeline arm. entity_connection_stats_windows is the 16-window stats arm behind the FIX-1117 source-fingerprint gate, and it is precisely the arm most likely to be skipped, deferred, budget-cancelled, or left mid-cycle — measured on prod 2026-09-01, one window ran 1810 s and inserted ZERO rows, and the cron-budget watchdog cancelled ec-crawl twice that day at 1915 s and 2087 s. So the MV can legitimately be STALE at read time, and today the live aggregation hides that from users. Selecting the MV would surface pipeline lateness as WRONG COUNTS on the page, which is a materially different failure mode from slow counts. WHAT TO MEASURE, on prod: (1) the actual request-path cost of the _conn.c aggregation today, from pg_stat_statements filtered to pgrst_source per [[reference_pgss_rpc_cost_audit_gotchas]], since if it is not actually expensive there is nothing to buy; (2) the observed staleness distribution of entity_connection_stats_mv — how far behind does connection_count actually run, across a normal week and across a bad day; (3) whether the two values agree today, which is the cheap falsification step and should be done FIRST because if they already disagree the premise is dead. Decide only after (1) through (3). If it proceeds, the read path needs the live-compute fallback convention from packages/db/CLAUDE.md rather than a bare MV read, and the freshness bound has to be stated as a product decision, not an implementation detail. Cross-ref [[FIX-1117]] [[FIX-1118]] [[FIX-775]] and the connection-counts MV swap precedent. <!--id:FIX-1132-->
- 🟠 M — **get_official_page completes above the anon 3s bound — anon visitors get a cancel where authenticated ones do not** — From the FIX-1128 bounds table (docs/audits/2026-09-13-fix1128-bounds.tsv). get_official_page is granted EXECUTE to anon AND authenticated, so it runs under two different real bounds: 3s for anon, 8s for authenticated. Prod pg_stat_statements via pgrst_source since the 2026-09-08 15:12 UTC baseline: 578 calls, mean 340.5ms, max 4608.0ms, stddev 913.1ms. pgss records only statements that COMPLETE — a cancelled one leaves no row, proved by the FIX-1128 probe — so 4608ms is a completion, and any anon request on that tail was cancelled with 57014 and left no trace in pgss at all. The stripped proconfig claimed 5s, which is looser than the anon bound and reads as permission it never had. The stddev is 2.7x the mean, so the tail is not rare. Fix shape per rule 20/32 is materialization or moving the heavy part off the request path, not a timeout. Measure the anon-vs-authenticated split first (the 57014s are only visible in the Logs API, not pgss). See [[FIX-1128]]. MEASURED by cc-137 (2026-09-19), and THE PREMISE OF THIS BULLET IS WRONG IN ONE WAY AND UNDERSTATED IN ANOTHER. There is no anon-vs-authenticated split to measure: the page calls the RPC through createPublicClient() (publishable key, persistSession false, no cookie handling) at apps/civitics/app/officials/[id]/page.tsx:353, so EVERY visitor is anon whether logged in or not, and pgss agrees - 366 anon calls and zero authenticated since the 2026-09-15 15:09:48 epoch. The Logs API cannot answer the split either way: a PostgREST cancel row's user_name is always authenticator (38 of 38 over 24h) because Postgres logs the LOGIN role, not the SET ROLE target, and there is no duration field to infer it from. THE RATE, epoch-aligned so pgss and the log are comparable: 80 cancels against 366 completions over 3.50 days = 17.9 pct (11.2 pct excluding the 09-16/09-17 event window). get_official_page is the single most-cancelled statement on prod. THE HARM IS WORSE THAN THE BULLET SAYS: withDbTimeout's 5s is a client-side Promise.race and the server bound is 3s, so the server always wins and the wrapper's data destructure renders the page as a real official with an empty record - no error, no 500, nothing but the 57014. THE CAUSE IS NOT WHERE IT LOOKS: the spending section scans financial_relationships with no official filter at all and costs 56 buffers (index-backed top-10). The cost is recent_votes at 6,034 of 13,272 buffers, because ORDER BY voted_at DESC NULLS LAST cannot use votes_official_voted_at (DESC NULLS LAST is not the reverse of ASC NULLS LAST), so it bitmap-scans ALL of an official's votes and top-N sorts to 100 - work proportional to the whole voting history, not to the 100 rows returned. voted_at has ZERO NULLs (0 of 969,302 clone, 0 of 982,966 prod). Also note the cohort axis: this RPC has no donor section, so cost tracks VOTE COUNT not donations - Kamala Harris is rank 1 by donations and the cheapest official measured (3,670 buffers); 408 officials have over 1,000 votes, max 2,530. DECISION FOR CRAIG, none taken here: (A) add index votes (official_id, voted_at DESC NULLS LAST) - exact semantics, no RPC change, ~16MB against a 338MB heap, worst clone case 14,882 to 7,702 buffers (-48 pct); (B) drop NULLS LAST from the inner ORDER BY and use the existing index - zero new bytes, identical results while voted_at has no NULLs; (B-prime) (B) plus SET NOT NULL, exact by construction, needs a validation scan. RECOMMENDED (A) - the only one that cannot change which rows a page shows. all_votes (3,556 buffers, 500 rows per page-view) is the second lever and IS a materialization question. STAYS OPEN - a measurement does not earn a close, and the build was out of scope. Full read in docs/audits/2026-09-19-fix1180-1181-bounds-read.md. Cross-ref [[FIX-1128]], [[FIX-1181]], [[FIX-646]], [[FIX-683]]. CLOSED 2026-09-20 (cc-138) on option (A), the one that cannot change which rows a page shows. votes_official_voted_at_desc (official_id, voted_at DESC NULLS LAST) was built CONCURRENTLY out-of-band on prod at 18:50 UTC in 8.55 s — 16 MB, indisvalid = t, exactly the size cc-137 measured on the clone — with the idempotent CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS twin pushed straight after and correctly no-opping (NOTICE 42P07, already exists, skipping), the FIX-1118 pairing working as designed. get_official_page's body was NOT touched. PROOF, same statement either side on prod: before, Limit (cost=2342.48..2342.73) -> Sort (voted_at DESC NULLS LAST) -> Bitmap Heap Scan on votes (cost=22.00..2263.22); after, Limit (cost=0.42..111.25) -> Index Scan using votes_official_voted_at_desc. The sort is gone and the estimate falls 21x. On the clone the whole RPC for Warnock measures 7,998 buffers against 13,274 before — 39.7 percent off, within 9 buffers of the 7,989 cc-137 predicted. GATES at start (18:50:44 UTC): 90.7 min since the last unguarded VACUUM's end, next not until 03:00 Monday; prod_session_state() clear, no live writers; ec_crawl_gate() clear with the donations arm in cycle_cooldown; zero transactions older than 2 min, so CONCURRENTLY waited behind nothing. The ASC index was deliberately NOT dropped — it carries 32,715,183 scans on prod and serves range predicates the DESC one does not, and dropping an index is a separate destructive decision. The empty-page half of this bullet was filed and closed the same run as [[FIX-1202]]. all_votes (3,556 buffers, 500 rows per page view) remains the second lever and is a materialization question, not this one. <!--id:FIX-1180-->
- 🟠 M — **Two service_role RPCs COMPLETE past the measured 8s PostgREST bound — the bound is not what it measures as** — From the FIX-1128 bounds table. The real bound for an impersonated service_role RPC through PostgREST was MEASURED on prod 2026-09-13 with _fix1128_probe_sleep (no proconfig): 7s returned OK in 7426ms, and 9s/10s/10s were each cancelled with 57014 at ~8.1s wall — authenticator's statement_timeout=8s, inherited across SET ROLE because service_role has no rolconfig of its own. Yet prod pg_stat_statements, userid=service_role, query via pgrst_source, since the 2026-09-08 15:12 UTC baseline, shows two statements that COMPLETED past it: link_officials_to_districts 5 calls mean 9895.4ms max 12402.8ms, and refresh_primary_source_for_entities 479 calls mean 107.4ms max 9835.7ms. pgss does not record cancelled statements (proved: the probe's cancelled 10s call left no row, its 2s control did), so these are genuine completions, not truncated timings. The proconfig is NOT the explanation — a looser proconfig cannot extend a tighter session bound, measured on the clone (2s session, 30s proconfig, cancelled at 2.001s, identical to no proconfig). Candidate: a pooled authenticator connection on which something issued a non-LOCAL SET statement_timeout, which every later request on that connection would inherit. Worth settling because it means the request-path bound is not uniform and cannot be reasoned about. Filed as ONE item rather than one-per-function because both are the same phenomenon. See [[FIX-1128]]. SETTLED by cc-137 (2026-09-19) as NOT A LIVE BUG, and the bullet's candidate is refuted. THE BOUND IS REAL AND service_role INHERITS IT: pg_db_role_setting has NO row for service_role at all (anon 3s, authenticated 8s, authenticator 8s + lock_timeout 8s, postgres IN DATABASE 3h), so its effective bound is authenticator's 8s taken at LOGIN and unaffected by SET ROLE - which is exactly what FIX-1128's probe recorded on 2026-09-13. THE CANDIDATE IS REFUTED: every session-scoped SET statement_timeout in the repo (direct-pg-upsert 48/75, heavy-rebuild 115/155/224/285/313/351, prod-session 533, session-lock 102, littlesis/matcher 171) is on a pg.Client or Pool the code constructs itself and closes in a finally; none is a PostgREST connection, and node cannot reach that pool with a SET at all since it is a separate process. fr-rewrite-drain:95 is SET LOCAL and moderation-harness:176 is set_config(..., true); apps/ has ZERO executable sites; withDbTimeout is a client-side Promise.race that issues no SQL. So no code change was made and none is warranted. IT STOPPED HAPPENING, AND THE RESTART IS WHEN: pgss stats_reset 2026-09-15 15:09:48.238 and pg_postmaster_start_time 15:09:48.333 are 95ms apart, so the epoch is a RESTART. Since it: link_officials_to_districts 1 call max 7,419.1ms, refresh_primary_source_for_entities 240 calls max 7,577.0ms - 241 completions, NONE past 8s, against 12,402.8ms and 9,835.7ms before it, and link_officials_to_districts appears in the cancel log in the same period so it IS being bounded now. A pgss row is genuinely a completion: measured on the clone, a statement killed by statement_timeout leaves ZERO pgss rows, so the old numbers were real over-bound completions. TWO MECHANISMS TESTED AND BOTH REFUTED: none of the three functions has an EXCEPTION block, WHEN OTHERS, or any mention of query_canceled (so the caught-cancel-disarms-the-timer path is out), and only search_path is in proconfig (and FIX-1128 already measured that a proconfig cannot loosen). REMAINING EXPLANATION, unconfirmable retroactively because pgss was wiped by the same restart and pg_db_role_setting keeps no history: a login-time role GUC is frozen on a pooled backend for its life, so PostgREST backends that logged in before the setting existed kept the old bound until the restart recycled them. Competing account that cannot be excluded: the platform changed authenticator's setting at or near the restart. THE READ THAT SEPARATES THEM GOING FORWARD, and it is what this bullet should now carry: pgss by userid for these two functions, re-read weekly - a service_role completion past 8s WITHOUT an intervening restart refutes the pooled-backend account; a clean week with the pool churning normally is the receipt. Monday is the first. Full read in docs/audits/2026-09-19-fix1180-1181-bounds-read.md. Cross-ref [[FIX-1128]], [[FIX-1180]], [[FIX-586]], [[FIX-462]]. Weekly read #1 (cc-141, 2026-09-21 ~23:45 UTC). pgss epoch: pg_stat_statements_info.stats_reset = 2026-09-15 15:09:48.238+00 — the crash-recovery restart itself — so the window is 6 d 8.5 h, and it independently corroborates pg_postmaster_start_time (15:09:48.333). Across the two RPCs: 600 service_role completions, max_exec_time 7,577 ms, mean 104 ms; postgres 2 calls, max 94 ms. NO completion past the 8,000 ms bound, so no recurrence — but 7,577 ms sits within 5 percent of it. Three clean weeks still owed. <!--id:FIX-1181-->
- 🔴 S — **financial_relationships carries multi-day visibility-map decay after a bulk rewrite: --defer-tails hands its VACUUM to a WEEKLY owner and autovacuum sits below threshold** — Measured on the FIX-1187 set-1 prod apply (cc-129, 2026-09-17). The merge rewrote financial_relationships (40,649 survivor-side deletes + 58,568 dup-side deletes + 40,649 moved) and --defer-tails deferred its VACUUM to the scheduled owner. That owner is fr-vacuum-analyze, schedule 0 1 * * 1 — WEEKLY, Monday 01:00. Post-apply FR sat at 204,091 dead tuples and 82.7 percent all-visible (631,265 relpages, 522,325 relallvisible), down from 100.0 percent. Autovacuum cannot collect it: the per-table autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor is 0.02, so the trigger is 50 + 0.02 x 14,469,877 = 289,448 dead tuples and the table is below it. So a bulk FR rewrite on any day but Sunday leaves the table degraded for up to six days with nothing able to collect it — the exact FIX-884 shape where all-visible loss turns index-only scans into per-row heap fetches, and precisely what the FIX-943 standing rule exists to prevent. Contrast EC and FE, whose owners are DAILY (04:30 / 04:50) and which recovered within the hour. Running VACUUM inline is not the answer — the script banner records that a script-run VACUUM of these tables is itself a front-door incident (FIX-1144). Options: give FR a daily vacuum slot like EC and FE got in FIX-1152; or have a bulk-rewrite script schedule a one-shot FR vacuum into the next daily slot; or lower FR autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor so a rewrite of this size trips it. Evidence in docs/audits/2026-09-17-fix1187-prod-apply.md section 4. Reading on 2026-09-17 23:23 UTC, one day after set 1's bulk rewrite: financial_relationships is at 212,083 dead tuples / 82.74 percent all-visible, with last_vacuum NULL and last_autovacuum NULL — matching the ~204k / 82.7 percent this bullet was filed on, so nothing has collected it in the intervening day and the Monday 01:00 fr-vacuum-analyze is still the first owner. For contrast on the same read, entity_connections sat at 457,118 dead / 73.31 percent (its own 04:30 daily owner had last run at 05:19:41) and financial_entities at 85,556 dead / 99.89 percent. cc-131 (2026-09-18, 03:59 UTC): financial_relationships carries 212,083 dead tuples at 82.7 percent all-visible, 77,365 below its autovacuum trigger (threshold 50 + scale 0.02 x 14,469,877 = 289,448), with its WEEKLY owner three days out - fr-vacuum-analyze next fires Monday 2026-09-21 01:00, and set 2 will add 46,798 rows of churn on top before then. A trap worth recording: FR reads vacuum_count 0 and last_vacuum NULL, and that is NOT a missing vacuum. The postmaster restarted 2026-09-15 15:09:48 UTC and pg_stat_statements_info.stats_reset carries that exact timestamp while pg_stat_database.stats_reset reads NULL, so every cumulative counter restarted then; fr-vacuum-analyze last ran 2026-09-14 01:00 in 200.7 s, before the restart, and EC's vacuum_count of 2 (09-16, 09-17) and FE's of 1 (09-16 only) corroborate the same epoch. Believing the counter without that check would have filed a false alarm here. EC meanwhile sits at 73.3 percent all-visible with 457,118 dead - decayed from the 100.0 percent its 09-17 daily recorded at 06:00, and static across two readings 70 minutes apart, so the daily 04:30 vacuum owns it. APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-132, THE OWNER IS NOW DAILY; the FIX closes on its receipt, not here): option 1 taken — migration 20260920000000_fix1191_fr_vacuum_daily.sql moved fr-vacuum-analyze from 0 1 * * 1 to 0 3 * * * by cron.alter_job keyed on NAME, applied to prod 2026-09-19 19:36:28 UTC (jobid 38 on prod, 55 on the local clone — FIX-946's point in one line), pending 1 to 0, verified after as jobid 38 / 0 3 * * * / active with the first firing 2026-09-20 03:00 UTC and 90 minutes of clearance ahead of ec-vacuum-analyze at 04:30. The slot was sized against four read constraints, not assumed: nightly.yml's worst observed drop-night tail is 2026-09-14 00:31 UTC (weeknights end 23:04-23:38), so 03:00 clears it by 2h29m; 04:15-05:00 already holds abuse-events-retention, ec- and fe-vacuum-analyze and FIX-1152's sequential-not-concurrent rule applies to a third table too; FIX-1124's startup-timeout histogram puts hour 03 at 0.0 percent against 9.2 percent for the daily's own 06:00; and the nearest daily neighbour vote-stats-refresh at 03:30 sits 26 minutes clear of a 227.2 s worst measured wall (fr-vacuum-analyze's last 30 days: 4 firings, min 11.6 s, median 110.5 s, max 227.2 s, zero failures). Note the prompt's fallback slot 30 3 * * * is NOT available — vote-stats-refresh already occupies it exactly. THE NUMBERS AT PUSH TIME, and they are worse than this bullet was filed on: measured 2026-09-19 17:46-18:45 UTC, entity_connections and financial_entities both read 0 dead / 100.00 percent all-visible on their daily owners while financial_relationships read 212,083 dead / 82.74 percent on its weekly one — same instance, same instant, and FR is written LESS (124,715 deletes this epoch against EC's 1,450,064), so cadence is the whole difference. The set-2 apply then took FR to 279,777 dead / 75.37 percent at 19:20:28, cutting autovacuum headroom from 77,365 to 9,671 against the ~289,448 trigger, with the weekly owner still 29.7 hours out — which is why the apply was deliberately ordered BEFORE the migration. CORRECTION to this bullet's own epoch note, which cc-131 got right and the cc-132 prompt compressed wrong: the instrument for the restart is pg_postmaster_start_time(), which reads 2026-09-15 15:09:48 UTC. pg_stat_statements_info.stats_reset carries the same timestamp but governs a DIFFERENT subsystem, and pg_stat_database.stats_reset — which is what actually governs pg_stat_user_tables — is NULL, meaning never reset. So the correct reading of vacuum_count 0 is not 'the counter was reset' but the harsher 'no vacuum of any kind for 4 days 2 h 41 m', with cron.job_run_details (a table, so it survived) independently recording the last firing at 2026-09-14 01:00:00 to 01:03:20. RECEIPT STILL OWED: the first daily firing after a rewrite that brings FR back to >= 99 percent all-visible — Sunday 2026-09-20 03:00 UTC, to be read Monday. Cross-ref [[FIX-1201]] for the operator banner this change just made stale. RECEIPT PAID (cc-141, 2026-09-21): two readings, both 100 percent. Reading 1 = 2026-09-21T00:27 UTC, after Sunday's 03:00 firing (212.0 s) and the ~1M-row drop-night emit: 100 percent all-visible / 67,144 dead. Reading 2 = 2026-09-21T23:37:53 UTC, after Monday's 03:00 firing (161.1 s): relpages 631,265 / relallvisible 631,265 = 100.00 percent, n_dead_tup 0, last_vacuum 09-21 03:02. The vm_probe:pre-fr row at 02:55 shows FR was ALREADY at 100 percent BEFORE Monday's vacuum, so the daily slot now pays 161 s to clear dead tuples rather than to repair visibility-map decay — the steady state this bullet asked for, and a direct input to [[FIX-1169]]'s gate-it-on-pg_class question. <!--id:FIX-1191-->
- 🟠 M — **merge-same-person-official-dupes _collision builds from the SURVIVOR side and costs 97 minutes on prod for a 151-pair manifest — the stub side is the cheap direction** — Measured on the FIX-1187 set-1 prod apply (cc-129, 2026-09-17). Of set 1's 115-minute merge transaction, roughly 97 minutes were a single statement: the _collision CREATE TEMP TABLE AS at merge-same-person-official-dupes.ts:2341-2354. It joins _manifest to financial_relationships as the SURVIVOR (s.to_id = m.survivor) and then to financial_relationships as the dup. The survivor side is the large one — 151 sitting members, several holding tens of millions of dollars — so the plan materialises the expensive direction. The backend sat on IO / DataFileRead for the whole 97 minutes. The same join computed READ-ONLY driving from the STUB side (99,217 rows, fr.to_id = m.dup, then probe the survivor) returned the identical collision census — 99,217 collisions, 8,456 amount-differing, 58,568/40,649/0 direction split — in a few minutes on the same instance. The ANALYZE _manifest that FIX-1165 added fixes the cardinality estimate but does not change which side is materialised, and FIX-1165's own comment already records a 200x prod/clone ratio on this statement for a two-official pair. This is why cc-128's 16-52 minute projection was wrong by an order of magnitude: it named FR move to_id as the step to watch, and FR move actually ran 265.8s. Worth re-shaping the join to drive from the stub side (or adding an explicit ordering hint) before any further set is attempted — set 2 was never applied partly because the box could not afford a second long merge. Evidence in docs/audits/2026-09-17-fix1187-prod-apply.md sections 2.1 and 5. FIXED in code by cc-130 (no trailer — it closes on the next prod apply that runs through this path). The CTAS now drives from the stub side. Output identity PROVEN, not argued: both forms built in one transaction on the local prod-clone against the committed 151-pair manifest return 98,878 rows with a symmetric difference of 0 in both directions across the full eight-column contract, and identical per-type censuses (donation 97,964 / ie_support 676 / ie_oppose 238). The cache-independent win is buffer counts, from EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) warm: the driven leg goes from 573,884 rows / 252,812 block reads (survivor-driven) to 99,217 rows / 54,429 reads (stub-driven) — 82.7 percent fewer rows, 78.5 percent fewer reads; 99,217 is exactly the stub FR count cc-129 measured on prod for set 1. Clone WALL is deliberately NOT quoted as the win: cold-first the old form took 51.0 s against 12.7 s, but with the order reversed it was 9.2 s against 7.3 s, so most of that first gap was cache residency. Three structural tests lock it, including one verified to FAIL when the join is flipped back. cc-131 (2026-09-18): still open - the closure condition is a PROD set driven through the stub-side _collision leg, and set 2 did not run. The clone dry run on the v2 four-row manifest exercised the leg again and completed with conservation $0 (22,233 survivor-side colliding losers deleted, 23,944 FR rows moved onto survivors, 21,907 stale EC money edges removed), but the clone cannot stand in for the prod wall this bullet is about. The collision direction is confirmed OPPOSITE to set 1's, as cc-129's manifest header predicted: 22,228 donation pairs with dup-fresher 22,204 against survivor-fresher 8. CLOSED 2026-09-19 (cc-132, measured on prod rather than inferred): the stub-side rewrite landed before set 2 and set 2 is its receipt. pg_stat_statements holds BOTH forms side by side in one epoch (the 2026-09-15 15:09:48 restart), which is the cleanest possible comparison — the survivor-driven form CREATE TEMP TABLE _collision ... SELECT s.relationship_type ... at 5,268.7 s (87.8 min, set 1's 151-pair / 99,217-stub-row manifest) and the FIX-1192 form, which pgss keys by its leading comment '-- FIX-1192 — drive this join from the STUB (m.dup) side, not the survivor', at 110.1 s (set 2's 4-pair / 46,798-stub-row manifest). A third row, the same old form at 364.5 s, is a separate invocation. Normalising for manifest size that is roughly 22x cheaper per stub row, on the statement that was 87.8 minutes of a 115-minute transaction. The bullet's claim that the predicates are symmetric and only the materialised side changes is confirmed by the result set: set 2's collision census (donation 22,228 pairs, dup-fresher 22,204, survivor-fresher 8, ties 16, $34,533,914 loser dollars) is identical between the clone dry run on the old plan shape and the prod apply on the new one. <!--id:FIX-1192-->
- 🟠 S — **A cancelled get_official_page renders as a real official with an empty record — the data swallow hides 57014 from the visitor and the crawler** — Measured by cc-137 (2026-09-19): 80 of 446 get_official_page calls over 3.50 days were cancelled — 17.9 percent (11.2 percent excluding the 09-16/09-17 event window) — making it the single most-cancelled statement on prod. Every visitor is anon (the page calls the RPC through createPublicClient, so there is no authenticated cohort with a longer bound), and withDbTimeout(..., 5000) is a CLIENT-SIDE Promise.race that issues no SQL, so it always loses to the server's 3s anon bound and cannot even report the cancel as a timeout. The page then destructures { data }, shims data ?? {}, and every section renders its empty state — so a cancelled page is indistinguishable from the FIX-683 recordless-shell skip: a real official, right name, right party, right photo, with no votes, no spending, no career, no promises. No error, no 500, nothing but a 57014 in the logs. Two harms: a visitor is told this official has no record, and a crawler is told the same and may cache it. The fix is to classify the three outcomes (skipped / ok / failed) before the shim, render a visible degraded state on failed, noindex that render, and mark it no-store so a transient cancel is never cached as an empty official. Cross-ref [[FIX-1180]], [[FIX-683]], [[FIX-646]]. FILED AND CLOSED 2026-09-20 (cc-138). classifyOfficialPage() separates skipped / ok / failed BEFORE the data ?? {} shim, and a failed render now gets: a banner saying the record could not be LOADED (not that it is empty), robots noindex so a crawler cannot cache the claim, noStore() so the 300 s ISR window does not hand a transient cancel to the next five minutes of visitors, and one greppable log line carrying the official id and the SQLSTATE. Deliberately keyed on error and NEVER on data == null — a content-bearing official whose RPC answered with nothing to show must not get a red banner saying the read broke, because it did not; the test pins both directions, three failure shapes (57014, client timeout, PGRST202) against three quiet-but-fine shapes, since a classifier returning failed for everything would satisfy the failure cases alone. WHAT IT COSTS, stated rather than buried: robots can only be set from generateMetadata, which runs before the page body, so the RPC moved into a React.cache'd fetcher both can reach and therefore resolves BEFORE the page's Promise.all rather than inside it — sequential with the donor-rollup read on a cold render. [[FIX-1180]] having just cut this RPC 39.7 percent of its buffers is what makes that trade payable now. The FIX-683 skip path is untouched. <!--id:FIX-1202-->
- 🟠 S — **Curate the four NULL-committee industry overrides FIX-922 made addressable** — FIX-922 added the uuid-keyed arm; it inserted no rows on purpose, because a curation is Craig-gated. The four prod donors it unblocked, measured 2026-09-20 (fec_committee_id IS NULL, total_donated_cents > 0, industry-tagged), total 416,500 USD: a222d616-0487-48ac-af68-0ec7eca301ba Ridge Coal Legacy Trust 265,000 oil_gas rule; 8a1e3c6f-03b1-46d0-9f61-42693de6f154 Cumberland Energy Action Fund 135,000 oil_gas ai; ae4d58df-917c-47ef-80c9-0ebf8d096189 Pharmaceutical Care Management Association 15,000 health rule; dac6b3f9-aebc-406d-a587-01998408f03b National Rifle Association of America Political Victory Fund 1,500 lobby rule. The first two are oil_gas escapees of exactly the kind FIX-921 swept and its committee-keyed mechanism could not reach. Shape: a new dated TSV led by financial_entity_id (NOT a seventh column on the closed 2026-07-27 or 2026-07-28 audits, whose test parses positionally), plus a 20260801020000_fix923-shaped migration writing fec_committee_id = 'fe:' || the uuid. The CHECK makes the two mutually derivable so they cannot disagree. See FIX-922 and FIX-916. <!--id:FIX-1204-->

## GENERAL / CROSS-CUTTING

- 🟢 M — **Sybil ↔ brigade corroboration join (data-gated)** — PR3 follow-up to [[FIX-571]] (Sybil detection layer). Cross-reference the two shadow candidate logs: flag `brigade_candidates` clusters whose account sets intersect `sybil_candidates` clusters (a coordinated pile-on that is ALSO shared-infrastructure-linked is a materially stronger review signal than either alone). Shape: a read-only join/RPC over `account_ids` (both logs carry a gin index on that column) surfacing the overlap for human review; still detection-only, no consequence. EXPLICITLY DATA-GATED — do NOT build until the observe-only `abuse_events` log has real post-launch traffic AND `data:sybil-scan` has appended real candidates on prod (today both logs are empty; the corroboration set would be trivially empty). Depends on `ABUSE_EVENT_PEPPER` being set in Vercel (linkage is NULL-blind until then). Detection ≠ punishment; enforcement remains a separate human-gated decision. <!--id:FIX-881-->
- 🟡 M — **Alphabetical DISTINCT ON tie-break silently redistributes multi-tag donor dollars across eight surfaces** — **Filed, not fixed** — surfaced by the [[FIX-916]] curation work and deliberately left alone there, because changing it moves numbers on eight surfaces whose conservation proofs would all have to be re-derived, which is not something a curation PR should do quietly. When a donor carries more than one industry tag, every consuming surface picks ONE via `DISTINCT ON (...) ... ORDER BY ... tag` — i.e. **alphabetically**. That is not a neutral tie-break, it is a systematic gradient: measured 2026-07-27, `agriculture` sorts first so it exports exactly $0.00 and keeps 100% of its dollars, while `transportation` sorts last so it imports exactly $0.00 and loses 44.4% of its dollars to earlier-sorting co-tags. A donor tagged both `agriculture` and `transportation` is counted entirely as agriculture on every surface, for no reason other than the letter it starts with. **Affected surfaces:** `sector_affinity_rebuild_officials()`, `official_sector_dollars_mv`, `chord_industry_flows_mv`, `chord_industry_flows_for_official()`, `chord_top_pacs_for_official()`, `get_official_donors()`, `refresh_group_donor_rollup()`, `fetchIndustryTagsByEntityId()`, plus the [[FIX-873]] contract-flow bodies. **[[FIX-916]] removes most of what this was mishandling** — it collapses 96.4% of the multi-tag donation dollar mass ($328.4M of $340.8M across 478 multi-tag donating donors) down to exactly one tag at the source — so the residual is the unaudited multi-tag tail, and the honest fix here is a deliberate ranking (by tag confidence, then by the donor's own dollar concentration) rather than by spelling. Note the same alphabet already bit [[FIX-908]]: renaming `pharma` to `health` moved the key's position in the ORDER BY so `health` now beats `labor`/`legal`/`lobby` where `pharma` lost to all three, moving ~$41.6M between buckets on the next incremental refresh with no data change whatsoever. Cross-ref [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-873]] [[FIX-875]] [[FIX-908]] [[FIX-872]]. <!--id:FIX-918-->
- 🟡 S — **financial_entity_industry_overrides cannot address a donor with a NULL fec_committee_id** — Surfaced by the [[FIX-921]] oil_gas sweep. public.financial_entity_industry_overrides is keyed on fec_committee_id (PRIMARY KEY), so any financial_entity carrying a NULL fec_committee_id is **structurally unreachable** by the curation mechanism — it can be neither re-assigned nor de-tagged, no matter what an audit finds. Two oil_gas escapees are in exactly that position and were dropped from the FIX-921 list for this reason alone: **Cumberland Energy Action Fund ($135k)** and **Ridge Coal Legacy Trust ($15k)**, both of which the audit judged mis-tagged. **Measured 2026-07-29 — the blind spot is SMALL:** 4 industry-tagged donating donors carry a NULL fec_committee_id ($416,500 total outflow), of which 2 are oil_gas. Small enough to leave the key as-is rather than revisit the table's PK, which is why this is filed rather than fixed — but it is a real hole and it will grow with any ingestion path that mints financial_entities without a committee id (IE-only spenders, USASpending recipients, IRS 990 orgs). **Suggested shape when it becomes worth doing:** add a nullable financial_entity_id column to the override table and make the resolver prefer fec_committee_id, falling back to the UUID, so the common case keeps its portable-across-re-seed key ([[FIX-916]]'s reason for choosing fec_committee_id) while the tail is still addressable. Do NOT swap the PK to the UUID — that is the portability the current key exists to protect. **REFRAMED 2026-08-01: this is an entity-DEDUP problem, not an override-key-coverage problem.** At least two of the four NULL-key donors are duplicates of FEC-keyed entities that already exist (verified on local 2026-08-01): National Rifle Association of America Political Victory Fund exists BOTH as C00053553 (`pac`) and as a NULL-keyed `other`-type row, and Pharmaceutical Care Management Association exists BOTH as C00388819 (`pac`) and as a NULL-keyed `other`-type row. The override mechanism already reaches the FEC-keyed twin; what is broken is that the NULL-keyed duplicate splits the donor's money and tags across two rows — the [[FIX-544]] merge class. The nullable-financial_entity_id column suggested above would ADDRESS the duplicate rather than merge it. Dedup first; only then see whether any genuinely FEC-less donor (the Cumberland/Ridge pair may be the same shape) leaves a residual blind spot worth a schema change. Cross-ref [[FIX-921]] [[FIX-916]] [[FIX-544]]. <!--id:FIX-922-->
- 🟡 S — **ai_summary_cache is never invalidated when the summary prompt changes — stale framing survives a prompt fix** — **File only.** Surfaced while shipping [[FIX-931]]. `ai_summary_cache` is keyed on `(entity_type, entity_id, summary_type)` and nothing in the key or the write path encodes **which prompt produced the text**, so editing a prompt has no effect on any already-cached summary — the old wording is served indefinitely. Concretely: cached summaries written before [[FIX-931]] still carry the pre-fix "Total raised" framing for official money, which [[FIX-931]] corrected precisely because it overstates what the FEC-itemized figure actually measures (it excludes unitemized small-dollar giving, joint-fundraising transfers, loans and other receipts). So the label on the page is now right and the AI prose next to it is still wrong, which is worse than either alone. Fix: add a **prompt-version component to the cache key** (a hash of the prompt template, or an explicit `prompt_version` column bumped when the template changes) so a prompt edit naturally misses the cache and regenerates, rather than requiring anyone to remember a manual purge. A targeted purge on prompt edit is the cheap interim, but it is the same class of "a comment/convention does not enforce" problem [[FIX-901]] documented — the durable fix is making the key carry the dependency. Check the same question for the tagger and narrative caches while in there. Note the regeneration cost is real (every affected entity re-summarised), so pair the key change with a count of what would invalidate before shipping it. Cross-ref [[FIX-931]], [[FIX-901]]. <!--id:FIX-938-->
- 🟡 M — **Decide the fate of the 6,888 pre-versioning summaries, and whether superseded text is retained** — Two questions FIX-938 deliberately deferred rather than answered. (1) Its migration backfilled all 6,888 ai_summary_cache rows (all proposal/plain_language; official/profile is EMPTY on both envs, so the pre-FIX-931 framing the bullet cited had no rows to invalidate) to the CURRENT version rather than 'legacy', because FIX-938 changed no prompt text and stamping them stale would force ~6,888 regenerations, roughly 11 USD at Haiku 4.5 rates against a 4 USD/month platform cap — blowing the cap and leaving the on-demand routes refusing on miss. The unproven part is that those rows actually match today's wording: they were written over months by three different producers. Deciding to re-stamp them 'legacy' is a cost decision, not a code change, and wants a budget window. (2) FIX-938 keeps ONE row per entity and overwrites on regeneration, so superseded text is not retained. If audit retention is wanted, the shape is a separate ai_summary_cache_history table, NOT a fourth column on the UNIQUE — the migration header records that 13 read sites exist, four of which cannot express the current key, and that versioning the key would blank the home page, the proposals index, proposal detail and entity search until regeneration. Related: packages/ai#generateSummary is dead code (no callers, only comment references from the two routes that reimplement it) and carries a summary_type vocabulary matching nothing in the table; delete it or make the routes use it. See FIX-938 and FIX-931. <!--id:FIX-1206-->

## OFFICIALS
- 🟡 M — **Cycle-scoped receipts need cycle_year on official_donor_rollup_mv** — Follow-up to [[FIX-931]], which relabelled the officials money cells to describe the number that actually exists rather than building a new one. official_donor_rollup_mv carries NO cycle_year column — its grain is (official_id, relationship_type, rank) over a per_donor CTE that SUMs across every cycle — so a cycle-scoped 'raised this cycle' figure is a **schema change, not a label change**: cycle_year would have to enter the MV grain (and the tail-bucket rank-1001 rollup recomputed per cycle), plus the officials page would need a cycle selector and a default-cycle policy. Note explicitly for whoever picks this up: the current all-cycle number is **correct-but-unscoped**, so a reader comparing an official's Civitics figure against an FEC per-cycle page will ALWAYS see a gap for that reason alone, entirely independently of the ingest defects in [[FIX-929]] / [[FIX-930]] and of the itemization floor. Do not conflate the two when triaging a 'the money is wrong' report — check whether the complaint is about scope (this bullet) or about coverage (the $200 per-transaction floor in indiv.ts and the missing oth{yy} joint-fundraising transfer ingest). Sizing note: official_donor_totals (FIX-836) has the same all-cycle grain and would need the same treatment if the headline figure moves onto it. <!--id:FIX-932-->
- 🟠 M — **Own-seat merge cannot take Al Green or Austin Scott — their stubs anchor external_relationships rows the merge has no step to re-anchor** — [[FIX-953]] merged 3 of its 5 class-B own-seat pairs. The two it refused are both almost certainly the same human — FEC files legal names, so **Al Green** (Representative TX-9) appears as *Alexander* Green [`H4TX09095`] and **Austin Scott** (Representative GA-8) as *James* Scott [`H0GA08099`], and both stubs' CAND_IDs encode the survivor's exact seat including district. They are blocked by the decision-5 rule 'a stub may carry money + provenance and NOTHING else', now mechanized as a hard refusal in `verifyOwnSeatInDb`: Alexander Green's stub anchors **6** `external_relationships` rows (3 from-side, 3 to-side: LittleSis `affiliated_with` / `business_partner` / `member_of` / `owns`) and James Scott's anchors **10** (9 from, 1 to, incl. 7 `appointment`). The merge moves `financial_relationships` and deletes only `donation`/`opposition` `entity_connections` edges, so those source rows would be stranded pointing at a $0 stub — the [[FIX-940]] lesson, where 'money and nothing else' was wrong by 1,755 votes. Austin Scott fails a SECOND, independent gate and should stay out regardless of how this is resolved: his stub shares **zero** (relationship_type, from_id, cycle_year) keys with his elected row — 0.0% donor-cycle overlap — and his elected row holds $0 across 0 FR rows, so there is no money evidence tying the two at all, only the seat. Options: (a) add a re-anchor step that repoints `external_relationships.from_id`/`to_id` (and any `entity_connections` derived from them) onto the survivor, which generalizes to the [[FIX-1019]] class-A backlog too; or (b) accept stranding and record it explicitly. NOTE the 15 stale `vote_yes`/`vote_no` `entity_connections` edges on Chuck Grassley's stub were NOT a blocker and did not stop his merge — the stub's `votes` count is 0, so those edges are orphaned artifacts of [[FIX-990]]'s dead rebuild, not data. Cross-ref [[FIX-953]], [[FIX-940]], [[FIX-990]], [[FIX-1019]]. **cc-128 (2026-09-16) measured the gate on prod: ZERO.** Across all 151 [[FIX-1187]] shared-CAND_ID stubs, every one of the eleven decision-5 attachment tables counts 0 on prod and 0 on the clone — votes, career_history, committee memberships, promises, cosponsorships, community comments, civic responses, lobbying disclosures, sponsored bills, proposal actions, external_relationships. So no 1187 pair is refused on this and the re-anchor step is not on 1187's critical path. The bullet stays open: the step still does not exist, and 1187's verifySharedIdInDb hard-refuses any stub that acquires an attachment before cc-129 applies. Re-measured on prod at apply time (cc-129, 2026-09-17): attachments across all eleven decision-5 tables are 0 on all 151 stubs, so this class was empty for the whole of [[FIX-1187]] set 1 and the merge never needed the re-anchor step. The bullet stays open because that step still does not exist. <!--id:FIX-1020-->

### Pipelines

- 🟡 M — **Surface attribution on graph viz routes — per-node + per-edge attribution semantics** — Deferred from [[FIX-398]] (2026-05-26). The attribution surface was wired into the four detail-page SSR loaders and a new /api/attribution/[type]/[id] route, but list endpoints and graph viz routes were intentionally out of scope because per-edge attribution semantics need their own design. Action: extend AttributionShape (or a per-edge variant) onto: (a) list endpoints — /api/search/entity, /api/graph/entities (per-row primary), (b) graph relationships — /api/graph/connections, /api/graph/spending (per-edge attribution: does a vote edge attribute to the proposal's source or the vote's roll-call source? does a donation edge attribute to FEC or to the recipient?), (c) graph viz aggregators — sankey, treemap-individuals, voting-divergence, chord (per-node attribution that survives aggregation grouping). Open design questions to resolve first: (1) per-edge attribution model — single source vs. (from_source, to_source) pair vs. relationship-typed source; (2) aggregator pass-through — when N edges collapse into one chord arc, what does attribution.primary reference? (most-common source? union? null?); (3) source_count cost on list endpoints — the cheap (primary + count) shape needs a per-row count subquery; benchmark before shipping, materialize as attribution_source_count on entity tables if it becomes a hotspot. Cross-ref [[FIX-398]] (predecessor), [[FIX-397]] (predecessor schema), [[FIX-399]] (SourceBadge), [[FIX-400]] (popover). <!--id:FIX-405-->
- 🟡 L — **Graph seeding for jurisdiction/institution/meeting results + unify graph groups with governing_bodies (brainstorm)** — Surfaced by the FIX-442 ship report 2026-06-03. The search Add-to-graph affordance forwards the result kind via /graph?addEntityIds=...&addEntityTypes=... (apps/civitics/app/search/components/SearchActionBar.tsx:24, AdvancedSearchPage.tsx:268), but the graph entity vocabulary is a closed union — official | agency | financial | proposal (apps/civitics/app/api/graph/entities/route.ts:205) — so jurisdiction/institution/meeting seeds arrive as types the graph cannot resolve (silent no-op or broken node). Two-part scope. (a) SHORT-TERM GUARD (S, can ship ahead of the brainstorm): hide/disable Add-to-graph for unsupported kinds, or strip them from the bulk-add payload, so users cannot seed types the graph will drop. (b) DESIGN SESSION (the real work, L): decide how the new types become graph citizens, resolving the existing groups/governing_bodies redundancy — graph groups today are URL-param-materialized official collections (groupId/groupName/groupIcon params on /api/graph/group, presets like Full Senate in apps/civitics/app/graph/EmptyStatePresets.tsx), while governing_bodies are DB rows describing essentially the same collections (US Senate gb = Full Senate preset). Open questions: does seeding a gb expand to its member officials (group semantics) or render the gb as a first-class node with member/jurisdiction edges (entity semantics)? Should the hardcoded presets be re-derived FROM governing_bodies rows so the two mechanisms collapse into one? Do jurisdictions seed as geographic group expansions (officials within the jurisdiction) or as nodes? Are meetings worth graph presence at all (likely defer)? What do entity_connections / get_connection_counts need for any new node types? Brainstorm needed before sizing — pair with the [[FIX-416]] gb-detail-page brainstorm (same how-do-gbs-surface-everywhere question). Cross-ref [[FIX-442]] (surfacing), [[FIX-416]] (gb brainstorm), [[FIX-405]] (graph per-edge attribution design, adjacent graph-design backlog). <!--id:FIX-468-->
- 🟢 S — **group_donor_rollup full refresh is ~6min on prod — top-N storage could cut it** — FIX-500's refresh_group_donor_rollup() full re-aggregate measured ~6min on prod under IOWait load (519,886 donor rows across 573 cohorts; the long pole is the INSERT + PK/read-index maintenance, seen as IPC/BtreePage waits). It runs off-path at the tail of the twice-weekly rebuild-entity-connections workflow (4h GHA budget, donations chunk alone ~90min), so 6min is ~1% of budget and NOT currently painful — but it is close enough to the 1800s hook timeout under heavier load to note. Cheap durable cut if it ever bites: store only top-N (e.g. 200) donors per cohort via ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY gb_id,party_key ORDER BY total_cents DESC) <= 200 — the route reads <=100, and group_donor_rollup_summary already carries the TRUE donor_count + full total_cents independent of stored rows, so top-N rows lose nothing the request path uses. Shrinks the table ~519k -> ~tens-of-thousands and makes the INSERT seconds. Gated follow-up from [[FIX-500]] decision-2 (full per-donor rows shipped as-is per 'don't pre-optimize; file a follow-up if painful'). <!--id:FIX-501-->
- 🟡 L — **Design: district geometry snapshot for voting-divergence + /api/districts** — DESIGN (do not implement in the quick-win session). Voting-divergence and /api/districts recompute district geometry/membership on the request path. Snapshot the geometry + per-district rollup into a table refreshed on the redistricting/import hook and serve reads from it (rolling-history snapshot shape per packages/db/CLAUDE.md). Reduces repeated heavy geometry scans against the cache-starved Pro Small. Follow-up to [[FIX-503]]. <!--id:FIX-508-->
- ⬜ L — **Graph edge full transaction ledger (paid tier — deferred design)** — DEFERRED DESIGN — filed by [[FIX-828]], NOT implemented this wave. The edge detail sheet (FIX-828) shows aggregate pair totals only. A future PAID TIER surfaces the full per-pair FEC Schedule ledger (individual transactions behind a donation/opposition/contract edge) with a free lookup limit (~10–25 lookups) before gating. Depends on the monetization phasing (credit/revenue mechanism must be live first — see the platform 'do not build AI/paid features before the credit mechanism' rule). Requires a new per-pair transactions API route, gating + free-limit metering, and paywall UI — none of which exist. Do not build until monetization phase. <!--id:FIX-830-->
- 🟢 M — **Saved views can't persist a hand-picked ids group (BrowseState is a predicate)** — [[FIX-886]] added `GroupFilter.officialIds` so BUNDLE AS GROUP means what it says, but an ids-group is **in-session only**: `BrowseState` is a predicate (scope + facets + q), and cannot express a hand-picked id list, so `compileBrowseToGroupFilter` never emits `officialIds` and the v2 saved-view payload has nowhere to put it. Consequences today: saving a view while an ids-group is in focus silently drops the group's membership, and a shared/reloaded session resolves nothing. Deciding this needs a call on semantics that [[FIX-886]] deliberately deferred — a saved id list is a SNAPSHOT (members who leave office, get merged by [[FIX-544]], or are promoted by `promote_candidate_to_elected()` would silently drift or 404), so the options are (a) persist ids with a resolution-time reconciliation pass that reports drops, (b) persist nothing and make the UI say ids-groups are session-scoped, or (c) materialize the selection into a real `user_custom_groups` row with an explicit membership table. The coverage manifest and a compiler test currently pin option (b) as the status quo — changing it must update both. <!--id:FIX-888-->
- 🟢 M — **Floterial overlay layer for the SLD choropleth (NH, 39 districts)** — FIX-914 seeded the 39 New Hampshire floterial House districts as real jurisdictions rows and taught query_districts() to keep them OUT of the bbox/state fill so a state choropleth stays a partition (design D7). They are therefore invisible on every map today, and the 58 representatives now linked to them are dropped from /api/graph/voting-divergence because their district_jurisdiction_id is not in the fill id set. Build the overlay: a second layer drawn on top of the base fill (outline or hatch, toggleable), fed by query_districts with the floterials INCLUDED. Two concrete traps for whoever picks this up. (1) voting-divergence pages query_districts per state at p_limit 200; NH lower is 164 base rows and 203 with the overlay folded in, so that cap MUST be raised in the same change or three districts vanish silently. (2) query_districts already returns overlay rows on the exact-p_id and point-containment paths; the fill exclusion is a single predicate in 20260910130000_fix914_query_districts_floterial.sql and the clean extension is a p_include_floterial boolean DEFAULT false, which changes the signature and so needs a DROP before CREATE or PostgREST sees two overloads. Overlay rows are identifiable by metadata floterial = true. NH is the only state with floterials in the residual, so this is one state problem today. Cross-ref [[FIX-914]], [[FIX-913]].  APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-135, CLOSED, prod): shipped as migration 20260919011500 (p_include_floterial boolean DEFAULT false appended last, DROP before CREATE as the bullet predicted, plus a floterial boolean output column so the client needs no second lookup) and the UI half in packages/graph/src/ChoroplethGraph.tsx. THE CHOROPLETH IS NOT IN GraphPage.tsx and it is NOT per-state -- it is packages/graph/src/ChoroplethGraph.tsx drawing a single nationwide geoAlbersUsa map, and voting-divergence loops all 51 states server-side to build it; the overlay is a second d3 layer (dashed outline, fill none, pointer-events on the stroke) over the base fill, with a role=switch toggle keyed on the response carrying overlay rows rather than on a hard-coded NH. FIX-914's D7 partition is preserved rather than relaxed: the default is still 164 for an NH lower fill on prod, and the reader test that pins it is unchanged. THE p_limit TRAP WAS ALREADY A LIVE BUG IN A STATE WITH NO FLOTERIALS -- PA's lower chamber is 203 BASE districts, so p_limit 200 has been silently dropping three Pennsylvania districts off this map since FIX-217, always the same three because query_districts has a deterministic ORDER BY; measured the largest per-state band (PA 203, NH 203 = 164+39, GA 180) and set 400. Local smoke on the nationwide sld_l band: 4,833 base fills + 39 overlay outlines, toggle 39 -> 0 with the base layer unmoved, and all 58 floterial representatives now carry a measure value where they previously had no row at all. <!--id:FIX-1170-->
