- 🟡 M — **official_homepage_stats_mv full-scans financial_relationships, and a covering index alone will not change that** — MEASURED under [[FIX-1133]], not built — see docs/audits/fr-index-census-2026-09.md. official_homepage_stats_mv's defining query reads every to_type='official' row of financial_relationships with no to_id predicate: SELECT to_id, count(*) FILTER (rt='donation'), count(*), sum(amount_cents) FILTER (...) FROM financial_relationships WHERE to_type='official' GROUP BY to_id. The prod plan is a Parallel Seq Scan of the whole 4,932 MB heap, filtering out 59.6% of rows. Note it does NOT ride financial_relationships_derivation, which is what the FIX-1133 premise assumed. THE MEASUREMENT, on the prod-scale clone (10.4M rows): the planner's Parallel Seq Scan costs 442,306 buffers (431,714 read) and 1,543.7 ms. A candidate covering partial index (to_id) INCLUDE (relationship_type, amount_cents) WHERE to_type='official' — 200 MB over 4.2M rows, about 250 MB on prod — gives a Parallel Index Only Scan at 182,716 buffers (36,828 read) and 628.8 ms. That is 2.42x on total buffers, 11.72x on physical reads, 2.45x on time. THE CATCH, and the reason this is a FIX rather than a one-line index build: THE PLANNER WILL NOT TAKE IT. With the candidate index present it still chose the seq scan. With enable_seqscan=off it chose a Bitmap Heap Scan on financial_relationships_to — 315,773 reads and 4,456 ms, WORSE than the seq scan it replaced. Only enable_seqscan=off AND enable_bitmapscan=off produced the index-only plan. So building the index on its own would add about 250 MB to an 18-index table (see [[FIX-1133]] — index bytes times index count is the vacuum budget), add one more sweep to every vacuum pass, and change nothing at runtime. THE WORK is therefore twofold: add the index AND make the daily refresh actually take it. Options, cheapest first: (1) set a session-scoped cost GUC in the refresh path only, which is narrow but fragile; (2) reshape the aggregate so the index-only path wins on cost honestly; (3) reconsider whether the MV needs the all-relationship-types count(*) at all, since the donation-only half could ride an existing sibling. Measure on PROD before committing — per [[reference_clone_understates_scan_bound_costs]] the clone understates scan-bound work. Cross-ref [[FIX-1133]] [[FIX-1142]] [[FIX-884]]. <!--id:FIX-1145-->
- 🟠 S — **nightly.yml starts 4.5-11.5h after its 02:00 UTC cron, and the FEC drop probe rides only in the fec-phase that the drift can kill** — nightly.yml is scheduled cron: 0 2 * * * but has actually STARTED between 06:43 and 13:51 UTC on every run since 2026-08-27 — 4.5 to 11.5 hours late. GitHub Actions queues scheduled workflows hardest at the top of the hour, and the FIX-903 header in packages/data/src/pipelines/fec-bulk/drop-check.ts already documents ~3.5h of that queueing as a known constant; the drift has since roughly tripled. TWO CONSEQUENCES, and the second is the one that costs data. (1) The start time is now unpredictable enough that the nightly can land inside windows it was placed to avoid — the 05:45-09:00 blackout the heavy-op rule protects, and the 06:00 UTC refresh-derived-mvs daily, which is the co-tenancy question FIX-1152 had to rule out by hand on 2026-09-06 (nightly 06:54:07 vs the daily 06:00:00-06:05:30, a 48-minute gap that happened to be clean and might not be next time). (2) The FIX-903 FEC drop probe (indivDropPending) runs ONLY inside the fec-phase job. A fec-phase that is killed at its timeout-minutes cap, or that never starts because the run queued past its own window, loses the day's collection AND the probe that would have noticed — the probe is downstream of exactly the failure it exists to detect. FIX SHAPE, two independent halves: (a) move the cron off the top of the hour to a :17-style minute, which is the standard mitigation for GHA scheduled-queue contention and costs nothing; (b) run the drop probe in the enrichment-light or tail job as WELL as in fec-phase, so a late or killed fec-phase still records whether a new indiv drop is pending. The probe is a best-effort HEAD that fails closed, so a second call site is cheap and cannot itself launch an ingest. BUILD THIS TOGETHER WITH [[FIX-1060]] — both are single edits to nightly.yml and landing them separately means two workflow-file churns for one file. Measured from gh run list on 2026-09-06 for the last ten scheduled nightly runs. Cross-ref [[FIX-903]], [[FIX-1060]], [[FIX-1156]], [[FIX-1011]], [[FIX-1152]], [[FIX-998]]. APPEND 2026-09-08 (SHIPPED, receipt pending). TWO CHANGES. (1) CRON: nightly.yml '0 2 * * *' -> '17 2 * * *'. Drift baseline, the ten scheduled starts to 2026-09-07 via gh run list --workflow nightly.yml --event schedule: 08:40, 07:57, 08:10, 07:21, 06:51, 06:55, 06:59, 06:43, 06:54, 07:03 UTC — 4h44 to 6h41 late, EVERY day, never once near the slot. Off-the-top-of-the-hour is the standard GHA scheduled-queue mitigation; the hour stays 02 so an on-time fec-phase lands ahead of the hour-04 vacuums and the 06:00 derived-MV daily instead of inside the 05:45-09:00 prod-read blackout the current drift puts it in. (2) THE PROBE IS NOW RECORDED FROM enrichment-light. Gate chain confirmed in the tree exactly as suspected: shouldProbeDrop = shouldLoadResumeState && !resumeStatePresent, and shouldLoadResumeState = runFec && !isWeekly && !held (fec-hold.ts:38, :47-52), so on the split-phase path the probe was skipped BY CONSTRUCTION in every phase but fec, and inside fec it is skipped on Sundays and whenever the hold is on. And the result was persisted NOWHERE: pipeline_state held only fec_bulk_cycle_watermarks and fec_indiv_watermark, and no data_sync_log metadata key mentioned the probe — the sole trace was a console line in a GHA log. New: probeIndivDrop() returns the full shape and indivDropPending() is now a one-line wrapper over it, so the fec-phase trigger's contract, shouldRunFecBulk's inputs, fecBulkHeldThisPhase and the hold are all byte-for-byte unchanged. recordDropProbe() writes pipeline_state.fec_drop_probe = {cycle, pending, remote_last_modified, watermark_last_modified, probed_at, phase}. The call site sits OUTSIDE the if (isWeekly) block on purpose — runEnrichmentLight's in-process stages are ALL weekly, so a probe placed inside it would record on Sundays only, the one day the drop is guaranteed stale. VERIFIED LOCAL: 4 new unit tests (17/17 in drop-check.test.ts, 495/498 suite-wide, 0 fail), including a matrix proving the light phase's runFec=false makes shouldRunFecBulk false for every (resume, drop) pair — a record-only call site cannot widen the trigger. A real light-phase run against local Docker logged the line and wrote the row: pending=true cycle=2026 remote='Sun, 06 Sep 2026 15:54:54 GMT' watermark='Sun, 16 Aug 2026 15:50:15 GMT'. The HEAD succeeded locally rather than failing closed, so the expected-degraded local path was not the one exercised. The Wed 09-09 enrichment-light job logged `[nightly] FEC drop probe (phase=enrichment-light): pending=false cycle=2026 remote=Sun, 06 Sep 2026 15:54:54 GMT watermark=Sun, 06 Sep 2026 15:54:54 GMT (FIX-1163)` and pipeline_state.fec_drop_probe holds the full row (probed_at 2026-09-09T07:36:00.979Z, phase enrichment-light, pending false) -- exactly the predicted steady state, recorded outside fec-phase as designed. Two starts read under the new cron on 2026-09-12: 09-10 22:51:49 UTC (+1h51m49s) and 09-11 22:54:15 UTC (+1h54m15s) -- both inside the 01:43-03:41 target window the cron move predicted, and both far tighter than the 4h43-6h41 band measured under 17 2. The Pattern B record commit is still owed and is not this one. APPEND 2026-09-14 (cc-119, CORRECTION TO THE 09-12 APPEND): the clause 'both inside the 01:43-03:41 target window the cron move predicted' is WRONG and is withdrawn -- 22:51:49 and 22:54:15 UTC are +1h51m49s and +1h54m15s after the 21:00 slot, and 01:43-03:41 was a superseded extrapolation from the 17 2 slot's 4h43-6h41 band, never a prediction for the 0 21 slot. What the four starts actually show is a 1h44-1h54 offset band that lands the fec-phase EARLIER than the target window, not inside it. <!--id:FIX-1163-->
- 🟡 S — **ec-vacuum-analyze is a fixed daily cost on quiet days — gate it on pg_class rather than running it unconditionally** — MEASURED ON PROD 2026-09-09. ec-vacuum-analyze (jobid 6, daily 04:30 UTC, added by FIX-1152) ran 124 s on Tue 09-08 and 133 s on Wed 09-09. Both are QUIET days: no Sunday FEC ingest, no remediation apply, no bulk rewrite. The read that matters is what it found — entity_connections at the 06:00 daily's vm_before probe was 98.7 pct all-visible with 14,352 dead tuples on 09-08 and 100.0 pct with 0 dead on 09-09, i.e. the table was already in the state the vacuum exists to restore, and the run still cost two minutes of prod IO inside the 04:30 slot. FIX-1152 sized these slots off the FAILURE case (the 09-04/09-05 dailies at 1,015 s and 1,012 s with EC at 79 pct all-visible) and the fix worked -- Wed's rebuild_entity_search_index unit came in at 241.1 s. The cost being questioned here is the floor, not the ceiling. DIRECTION: gate the daily EC vacuum on a pg_class-keyed predicate rather than the clock -- skip when relallvisible/relpages is above a threshold (95 pct is the obvious first cut, given the observed failure mode begins below 90) AND n_dead_tup is under the tuned autovacuum trigger. The state is already read every morning by check_rebuild_autovacuum_status() and recorded by the 06:00 daily's vm_before, so the predicate needs no new instrumentation. Keep the unconditional run on the day after any bulk rewrite -- that is the case the standing FIX-943 rule covers and it must not be gated away. NOT URGENT: two minutes a day inside a quiet 04:30 window is a small bill and the current job is CORRECT, merely unconditional. File-only from cc-117; no change made. Cross-ref [[FIX-1152]], [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-1133]], [[FIX-884]]. APPEND 2026-09-12 (THE SERIES NOW COLLECTS ITSELF): [[FIX-1176]]'s receipts file carries ec-vacuum-analyze and fe-vacuum-analyze durations in its section 4 every night, and docs/receipts/bands.json holds the <= 140 s quiet-day ceiling as a checked band rather than a remembered number. First prod reading through it, 2026-09-12: ec 9.0 s at 04:30 and fe 10.8 s at 04:50, both far under the 124-133 s the series opened with -- so the fixed-cost argument this bullet rests on needs re-reading against the newer numbers before the pg_class gate is built. APPEND 2026-09-14 (cc-119, SERIES THROUGH 09-13 PLUS A STAMP-SITE CORRECTION). Six days from the receipts files' section 4 -- ec 123.6 / 132.6 / 88.0 / 8.9 / 9.0 / 97.3 s and fe 107.9 / 10.9 / 15.6 / 10.9 / 10.8 / 16.2 s for 09-08..09-13; the 09-14 04:30 point is after this session. The cost is NOT fixed: ec swings 8.9 s to 132.6 s, a 15x range, and the two quiet days (09-11, 09-12) sit at ~9 s while 09-13 rebounded to 97.3 s. That is write-volume-keyed, which is the opposite of the premise this bullet was filed on, and it is also why a pg_class gate would save ~9 s on the days it fires and nothing on the days that cost real time. THE STAMP-SITE CORRECTION, and it undercuts the bullet's own evidence: vm_before is computed at refresh_derived_mvs RUN START, which for the daily is 06:00 UTC -- ninety minutes AFTER ec-vacuum-analyze at 04:30. So 'the table was already 100 percent all-visible before the vacuum' was read from a POST-vacuum probe and is not evidence about the pre-vacuum state at all (rule 107). Nothing in the tree measures entity_connections' visibility map immediately BEFORE the 04:30 vacuum, so the question this bullet asks is currently unanswerable from the instruments that exist. No gate built; the next move is an instrument, not a gate.  APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-136, context only, no change): a second table now asks this bullet's instrument question, and answers it in the opposite direction. entity_tags (see [[FIX-1178]]) is exactly the table a pg_class gate would correctly decline to vacuum -- 98.1 percent all-visible, 71,198 dead against a 268,044 trigger, autovacuum already firing 17 minutes after each bulk rewrite -- and yet it is the table whose daily job has crept +32 percent per row. So the gate this bullet proposes would be right AND the job would still be slow, because the cost is 1.72x heap bloat, which no vacuum decision reaches. Two consequences for the gate's design: the pg_class predicate should be stated in terms the reader can act on (dead-tuple headroom against the trigger, which is what a vacuum fixes), and the thing worth ALSO recording per run is a bloat ratio (relpages against reltuples x avg_width), which is what a vacuum does not fix and which no job currently stamps anywhere. APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-132, A SECOND EC SLOT WAS OFFERED AND REFUSED; the two numbers that refused it): cc-132 carried an option to add ec-vacuum-analyze-pm at 0 13 * * *, conditional on BOTH (i) the 04:30 job's 30-day median wall <= 140 s and (ii) EC accumulating >= 10k dead tuples/h between vacuums measured INSIDE the landing window. (i) PASSES: 17 firings in 30 days, median 123.6 s, avg 322.4 s, max 3,147.9 s (the 09-17 set-1 outlier), zero failures. (ii) FAILS: two readings 59 minutes apart inside the window, 2026-09-19 17:46:04 and 18:45:18 UTC, both read entity_connections at 0 dead tuples and 100.00 percent all-visible, 13.3 hours after its 04:31:39 vacuum — a rate of 0/h. No second slot was scheduled. BUT THE REFUSAL RESTS ON A SATURDAY, and that is a gate-design problem this bullet should own rather than a settled answer. The same instrument on the two preceding days reads ~20,500 dead/h (457,118 dead at 2026-09-18 02:49, cc-131) and ~12,300 dead/h (228,963 dead at 2026-09-18 23:07, from the receipts file's 'Visibility map now' block, which is stamped at generation time — 23:07 the evening BEFORE its nominal day — not at 06:00). Either would have passed (ii) comfortably. So the gate as written decides a seven-day schedule from whichever day the landing window happens to fall on, which is the same error class as cc-131's 03:00-trough reading one level up: the fix is not to loosen the threshold but to state which POPULATION the rate must be measured over (a weekday, or a trailing-7-day maximum), per rule 127. Two further notes for whoever builds the pg_class gate. First, this bullet's 2026-09-14 append is confirmed rather than superseded: EC's cost is write-volume-keyed, and the 0/h Saturday against 20,500/h Thursday is the sharpest instance of that yet measured. Second, and this is the instrument gap that matters more than the gate: cc-132's drain check found that a sub-second ec-crawl firing is AMBIGUOUS — ec_crawl_gate() returned run false / reason cycle_cooldown / 'last cycle closed 256 min ago, minimum interval is 360 min' at 19:33:21, and by FIX-1111's deliberate logging asymmetry (only backoff writes a data_sync_log row) that firing wrote no row at all, so entity_connections_rebuild shows a GAP at 19:30 where financial_entity_totals_refresh shows complete. A reader checking 'is EC caught up' from wall time or from data_sync_log cannot distinguish caught-up from cooling-down; the satisfiable form is ec_crawl_gate()'s own reason plus the entity_connections_donations watermark. APPEND 2026-09-20 (cc-138): THE INSTRUMENT LANDED, THE GATE DID NOT, AND THIS STAYS OPEN. The question this bullet asks — is a given vacuum run worth its fixed daily cost — could not be answered because nothing measured a table's visibility map immediately BEFORE its vacuum: FIX-1152's vm_before is stamped at refresh_derived_mvs run start, 06:00 UTC, AFTER all three vacuums, so it records what the vacuum LEFT and the decision needs what it FOUND. public.record_vm_probe(label, relations[]) now writes pipeline_state key vm_probe:<label> in FIX-1152's exact vm_before shape, element for element — which is also the shape check_rebuild_autovacuum_status() publishes (FIX-885) — so the pre and post readings diff with no translation step. Three jobs are LIVE on prod, five minutes ahead of the vacuum each is paired with, schedules read off cron.job by NAME: vm-probe-pre-fr 55 2 * * * (financial_relationships), vm-probe-pre-ec 25 4 * * * (entity_connections AND votes), vm-probe-pre-fe 45 4 * * * (financial_entities). votes rides with the EC probe because cc-136 section 3.4 found it is the only Index Only Scan in the rule-tagger plan (96.7 percent all-visible) and it has NO vacuum owner at all, so its decay is the one nothing would otherwise notice. Each command is a single SELECT of a plain function that never COMMITs, the one shape for which the single-statement pg_cron form is safe (FIX-1128), and none carries a cron_job_budget row because two catalog index lookups plus an upsert cannot run long. Receipts section 4 renders a before-the-vacuum block per relation. FIRST READINGS: Monday 02:55 / 04:25 / 04:45 UTC. The gate decision waits for a week of them. One thing the build surfaced and did not fix: receipts' VACUUM_JOBS list is still ec- and fe-vacuum-analyze only, so section 4 does not show fr-vacuum-analyze even though FIX-1191 made it daily at 03:00 — the probe covers FR, the vacuum wall does not. First vm_probe rows read on prod (cc-141, 2026-09-21): pre-fr 02:55:00 financial_relationships 631,265/631,265 = 100.0 percent, 67,144 dead, last_vacuum 09-20 03:03; pre-ec 04:25:00 entity_connections 373,641/341,006 = 91.3 percent, 112,391 dead, AND votes 73,085/60,343 = 82.6 percent, 26,929 dead with last_vacuum NULL — a relation carried in the probe that no vacuum job owns; pre-fe 04:45:00 financial_entities 245,569/245,569 = 100.0 percent, 77,541 dead. Section 4 of the receipts now collects fr-vacuum-analyze and is titled 'Daily vacuums' (commit 9b1fc92c), closing cc-138 section 8(i) rather than parking it. Two inputs to the gate-it question: FR was already at 100 percent when its 03:00 vacuum fired, and EC decayed 91.3 to 76.44 percent between 04:25 and 23:37 the SAME day, so the two relations want opposite gates. The outage (15:10 to 22:28 UTC, [[FIX-1212]]) hit the small-table series instead: all seven 17:05-17:21 vacuums failed with job startup timeout. The 09-23 probes (02:55 / 04:25 / 04:45) are the first after the restart and were not yet due when cc-145 closed. <!--id:FIX-1169-->
- 🟡 S — **A supervised prod session longer than ~48 minutes reports financial_entity_totals_refresh stale, because a skipped firing correctly does not advance the freshness clock** — Found while building [[FIX-950]] (2026-09-11); not a defect in it, an unpaid interaction between two correct behaviours. check_rollup_freshness() and list_scheduled_rollup_pipelines() both count ONLY status='complete' ([[FIX-1140]]), so a firing that stands down for a supervised prod session writes 'skipped' and the freshness clock keeps running — which is the honest reading, nothing happened. But the thresholds are derived from each pipeline's own cadence at 1.5x (report) and 2.5x (escalate), and the fastest crawl arms have very short cadences. Measured on prod 2026-09-11 from list_scheduled_rollup_pipelines(): financial_entity_totals_refresh cadence 0.50h, reports at 0.8h, escalates at 1.3h; entity_connections_rebuild 7.75h/11.6h/19.4h; donor_rollup_refresh 12h/18h/30h; everything else 24h or slower. So a hold under ~45 min is invisible, a hold past 48 min produces a 'stale rollup: financial_entity_totals_refresh' report, and past 78 min an escalation — for a session that is doing exactly what it is supposed to. The FIX-950 receipt was sized at 35 min specifically to stay under it. The mechanism to fix it already exists and is already read by the census: rollup_watch_overrides.held_since / hold_reason, which list_scheduled_rollup_pipelines() consults to NULL out report_after_hours and escalate_after_hours. Fix shape: claimProdSession() sets held_since + hold_reason on the fast-cadence pipelines (or on all guarded pipelines) and clears them on release, in the same best-effort way it writes the pipeline_state label — never able to fail the claim. Open question worth deciding first: whether suppression should be scoped to the pipelines a hold can actually affect (the guarded set) or to all of them, since a hold does not stop a GHA-driven pipeline from being genuinely late for its own reasons. Documented meanwhile in docs/ops/prod-holds.md. Cross-ref [[FIX-950]], [[FIX-1140]], [[FIX-1011]], [[FIX-1135]]. MECHANISM BUILT by cc-133 (cd81917c, local); STAYS OPEN for the receipt. claimProdSession() upserts held_since/hold_reason for the GUARDED set only, derived at claim time from [[FIX-1190]]'s mapping - the open question in this bullet was decided that way (D1(a)): a hold cannot make a GHA- or Vercel-driven pipeline late for its own reasons, so suppressing a threshold a session cannot affect would make the hold a blindfold rather than a mute. No migration: list_scheduled_rollup_pipelines() already NULLs both thresholds on held and canary-check gates on !w.held. Measured on the clone: 16 rows, not 17 - entity_connection_stats_rebuild is retired and the upsert's retired_at guard correctly skipped it - all 16 reading back as held:true with both thresholds NULL, and zero session rows after release. CLOSES ON: a hold longer than 48 minutes on prod with no stale rollup: financial_entity_totals_refresh report and no escalation from the nightly canary. No qualifying hold yet (cc-141): no supervised prod session ran and none exceeded 48 minutes, so financial_entity_totals_refresh produced nothing to read.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, note only — no new evidence): same two dry-run holds as [[FIX-1177]]'s append — 55 s and 4 s, 16 guarded pipelines each, automatic release. Far below the ~48 min point at which financial_entity_totals_refresh would report stale, so check_rollup_freshness was not read and this run adds nothing to the case. Still waiting on a long supervised session.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, second reading — still no evidence): same three windows as [[FIX-1177]]'s append (38 s / 46 s / 60 s). financial_entity_totals_refresh was never at risk of reporting stale and was not read. <!--id:FIX-1172-->
- 🟠 S — **The 'cron' kill switch is dead — nothing reads pipeline_state.kill_switches.cron, and FLAGS.CRON_ENABLED has no consumer, but the admin UI offers both** — Found by enumerating the three hold mechanisms for [[FIX-950]]'s precedence doc (2026-09-11), which required answering 'what reads kill_switches.cron' by reading every caller rather than assuming. The answer is nothing. isKillSwitchEnabled(db, 'cron') is never called anywhere in apps/ or packages/ — the only live callers of that function pass 'mapbox_geocode', 'ai_summaries' and 'cf_auto_mitigation'. FLAGS.CRON_ENABLED in packages/data/src/feature-flags.ts is defined and has zero consumers. The env half, CRON_DISABLED=true, IS live, but only in two Vercel cron routes: apps/civitics/app/api/cron/nightly-sync/route.ts and apps/civitics/app/api/cron/notify-followers/route.ts. It does not affect the GitHub-Actions nightly and it does not affect a single one of the 38 pg_cron jobs. WHY THIS MATTERS RATHER THAN BEING TIDY-UP: 'cron' is listed in KillSwitchName and is therefore rendered and flippable from /api/admin/kill-switches alongside switches that DO work. An operator who flips it off expecting the platform's scheduled work to stop would be wrong, would get no feedback saying so, and would then be surprised by a rollup rebuild landing mid-incident. A dead switch that looks live is worse than no switch. Three candidate shapes, pick one: (a) delete 'cron' from KillSwitchName and the admin route, leaving CRON_DISABLED as the honest env-only control for the two Vercel routes it actually covers; (b) make it real by having the pg_cron guard family and the nightly consult it the way they now consult prod_session_state() — note [[FIX-950]] deliberately did NOT do this, because merging the three hold mechanisms was out of scope; (c) keep it and rename it to what it actually gates (vercel_cron_routes). (a) is the cheapest and the most honest; (b) is the most useful and is a real design decision about whether an indefinite operator-level platform-wide stop should exist at all. Documented meanwhile in docs/ops/prod-holds.md section 3. Cross-ref [[FIX-950]], [[FIX-311]]. CLOSED by cc-133 (1f4159a6, local) via option (a). Removed 'cron' from KillSwitchName, ENV_RULES and the admin route's ALLOWED_NAMES, and deleted FLAGS.CRON_ENABLED; CRON_DISABLED stays as the honest env-only control for the two Vercel routes. (b) and (c) stay unbuilt, and (b) deliberately so - whether an indefinite operator-level platform-wide stop should exist at all is a different question from removing a switch that never worked, and what exists today for stopping scheduled work is the supervised session, which is bounded by a live process rather than by somebody remembering to undo it. THE PART THAT WAS NOT DELETION: prod's pipeline_state.kill_switches row still carries a cron key and kill_switch_events still carries its history, neither migrated - rewriting a JSONB blob to satisfy a TypeScript union is a schema change in service of a type. That left two casts over old data, loadMap's and auto-trip-evaluator's Object.entries key cast, so both now go through a new filterKillSwitchesMap() parse boundary. OPERATIONS.md's CRON_DISABLED row said 'Stops nightly cron from running', which is the exact wrong belief this bullet is about, and was corrected. <!--id:FIX-1173-->
- 🟡 M — **Twenty-odd operator-run prod-writing scripts still do not claim the supervised prod session — FIX-950 wrapped only the five FR-rewrite landings** — Filed by [[FIX-950]] at its own boundary (2026-09-11). D3b says the safe path is the default path (rule 47) and withProdSession() implements it, but FIX-950 wired it into exactly the five scripts that call drainFrRewrite — remediate-role-ineligible-holders, remediate-cross-person-misattribution, remediate-fec-emit-residue, remediate-bound-cross-person, merge-same-person-official-dupes — because those are the family cc-119's Monday apply belongs to and the family whose contention FIX-1165 actually measured. Everything else in packages/data/package.json that ends ':prod' and writes is still on the honour system. The ones that write, from the 2026-09-11 census: data:merge-dc-jurisdiction:prod, data:merge-territory-jurisdictions:prod, data:merge-unicameral-legislatures:prod, data:merge-fe-collisions:prod, data:backfill-gb-seat-counts:prod, data:backfill-gb-attribution:prod, data:donor-rollup:sweep:prod, data:donor-rollup:bulk:prod, data:treemap:sweep:prod, data:backfill-donor-totals:prod, data:backfill-small-dollar:prod, data:backfill-vote-stats:prod, data:backfill-contract-flow-rollups:prod, data:backfill-sector-affinity:prod, data:backfill-agency-staffing:prod, data:backfill-treemap-individuals:prod, data:move-stub-senate-votes:prod, data:recategorize-complexity-tags:prod, data:backfill:sld-links:prod. The three donor-rollup/treemap sweeps are the most valuable of these to wrap, because they drive the very procedures the guards now cover and can therefore contend with a session that is itself driving them. IMPORTANT NEGATIVE: --allow-prod is NOT the right hook and must not be used as one. It is a low-level guard inside createAdminClient() and the SCHEDULED pipelines carry it too — data:nightly:ci, data:fec-bulk:ci, data:irs990:ci, data:usaspending-bulk:ci, data:audit:ci. Auto-claiming on --allow-prod would make the nightly claim the session and then read its own lock as held, skipping every writer phase it just claimed the box to run. The correct hook is the entry point of an OPERATOR-RUN script, which is what runUnderProdSession() takes. Fix shape: one line per script tail, the same edit FIX-950 made five times, plus a per-script expectedMinutes. Worth doing in one pass with a test that greps package.json for ':prod' scripts and asserts each named entry point calls runUnderProdSession, so the next one added cannot silently be outside the interlock. Cross-ref [[FIX-950]], [[FIX-1165]], [[FIX-1074]]. CLOSED by cc-134 (d9a1c030, local). The census from package.json rather than from this bullet: 41 :prod entries, 11 wrapped before (six files - the five FR-rewrite landings plus restore-fec-ids), 30 not. Twenty-one script files gained the FIX-950 entry-point wrapper in the existing shape, taking it to 35 of 41 entries; six entries are audited reads. expectedMinutes is from each script's own measured history where one exists (the clone's data_sync_log, restored from prod): donor-rollup 240 from donor_rollup_refresh's 188-minute max over 27 runs, treemap 90 from 46.3, vote-stats / agency-staffing / contract-flow 10 from 1.2 / 1.0 / 0.0; 30 where there is none, 5 for set-vercel-plan-pro, which is one upsert. THREE THINGS THE BULLET DID NOT ANTICIPATE. (a) Three dual-mode sweeps (donor-rollup-sweep, donor-rollup-bulk, treemap-global-sweep) share one file between a writer and a --status read, so the wrapper carries a deliberate --status bypass: a read that claimed the interlock would hold all seventeen guarded pipelines and REFUSE under another session, making the cheapest check the one you cannot run when you need it. (b) audit-cross-person-misattribution.ts said READ-ONLY in its header and is not - it runs a genuine UPDATE over the whole non-colliding population inside BEGIN ... ROLLBACK, which commits no data but writes a heap tuple per row, takes the row locks, and leaves them all dead; wrapped, header corrected. (c) grep over the .ts misses a writer whose SQL lives in a sibling .sql file - recategorize-proposal-complexity-tags.ts reads sql/recategorize-proposal-complexity-tags.sql and shows zero write verbs in its own source. prod-scripts-interlock.test.ts is the standing guard: every :prod key either names a file containing runUnderProdSession( or sits in an explicit READ_ONLY map with a one-line reason, the map is audited against package.json so a renamed writer cannot return under a stale exemption, and the hook is deliberately NOT --allow-prod because the scheduled :ci pipelines carry it and would self-lock. <!--id:FIX-1174-->
- 🟡 M — **CC prompts and reports live in the repo, and a report's claims are checkable** — Craig is the transport in both directions of the CC loop: he copies a Cowork prompt into Claude Code, and pastes CC's report back. Neither leg needs a human. Build: (a) .claude/cc.config.json names a promptsDir and reportsDir, both resolved against the MAIN checkout via git rev-parse --git-common-dir so they work identically from a session:worktree; (b) a /cc N slash command reads the newest cc-prompt-N-*.md from promptsDir, runs it, and writes its after-commit report to docs/cc/reports/cc-N.md; (c) the report opens with YAML front matter (cc, prompt, started_at, finished_at, head_before, head_after, commits, fixes_closed, fixes_filed, fixes_reopened, migrations_pushed, prod_writes, ci, stopped_items) which pnpm cc:verify N checks against git and done.log — every commit sha exists and is an ancestor of origin/main, head_after is on trunk, every claimed close derives CLOSED with a row naming one of this run's shas, every filed id has a marker, every reopened id derives OPEN on a reopen row, and ci green is cross-checked with gh when available; (d) docs/cc/PROMPT_TEMPLATE.md holds the invariant prompt sections and the rules checklist so a rule change is one edit instead of remembered each time; (e) a Stop hook runs fixes:check and cc:verify so a report whose claims the tree contradicts never reaches the chat. Status derivation comes from FIX-1016, whose fixes:status --json is what the verifier consumes. Cross-ref [[FIX-1016]]. <!--id:FIX-1175-->
- 🟡 M — **Daily receipts file: a read-only receipts:daily script and a nightly tail job commit docs/receipts/YYYY-MM-DD.md + .json** — The same dozen reads are banked by hand in every Cowork prompt — the nightly's createdAt vs its slot, per-phase status/duration/peak_rss_mb, the hour-04 vacuum durations, the 06:00 daily's unit_seconds and vm_before, a dozen pg_cron jobs against their bands, the FEC drop probe, the FIX-950 interlock's footprint, the canary's conditions, the SLD linkage residual. None of it needs a model. A read-only pnpm receipts:daily writes docs/receipts/YYYY-MM-DD.md (human) + .json (machine) with every number next to the query that produced it, verdicts computed against a hand-edited docs/receipts/bands.json (no band = no-band, never in-band), and a receipts job chained after enrichment-tail-phase in nightly.yml (if: always(), contents: write, docs-only push to main) commits it. Turns receipt = the next scheduled run into receipt = tomorrow's file and shrinks the no-prod-contact-while-a-receipt-is-taken window to the job's own duration. Nominal day = the slot's NIGHTLY_SLOT_OFFSET_HOURS rule from weekly-gate.ts, so the file is named for the day the run itself gated on. Pattern B: the first scheduled firing is the receipt. Cross-ref FIX-1163, FIX-1169, FIX-1152, FIX-950, FIX-1175.  APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-144, note only — closed, folded forward): the receipts writer has one more hazard than this bullet recorded, filed as [[FIX-1209]] and fixed there: the output path is named from the nominal day ALONE, so a LOCAL run and the nightly PROD run resolved to the same docs/receipts/YYYY-MM-DD.md and the local one silently overwrote the day's committed prod receipt with local-Docker numbers. cc-142 restored 2026-09-22 from HEAD by hand. A LOCAL run with no --out now writes under docs/receipts/local/ (gitignored); bands.json deliberately keeps resolving against the canonical directory so a redirected run does not silently read no-band for every job. <!--id:FIX-1176-->
- 🟡 S — **A FIX-950 interlock skip is invisible to every freshness reader — check_rollup_freshness and the FIX-1011 registry both count only status='complete'** — Read for [[FIX-1176]] Phase 0 (2026-09-12), answering the question cc-121 left open. Both readers filter on complete and nothing else. check_rollup_freshness (20260903060000_fix973b) derives last_complete_at / hours_since_complete / stale from a single last_good CTE, WHERE pipeline = p_pipeline AND status = 'complete'. list_scheduled_rollup_pipelines (20260903000000_fix1135_1059_1011) measures cadence over a closures CTE, WHERE a.status = 'complete', and closures_any likewise. So a firing that the FIX-950 prod-session guard turned into a status='skipped' row with skip_reason 'prod session held: …' is, to both, indistinguishable from a firing that never happened: the freshness clock keeps climbing and the cadence median is computed over the gap as if the job had missed it. THE TWO CONSEQUENCES ARE DIFFERENT SIZES. (a) Cosmetic and already handled: any census column built on the freshness verdict renders a deliberate skip as 'missing' — FIX-1176's receipts file sidesteps this by reading data_sync_log directly for status + skip_reason and emitting verdict skipped(reason) as its own value, never in-band and never missing. (b) The real one: a hold long enough to span escalate_after_hours (cadence x 2.5 — 60h for a daily rollup, ~420h for a weekly) makes the canary escalate ON A DELIBERATE OPERATOR ACTION, and the operator gets a red X for using the interlock correctly. No current hold is close (FIX-950's are minutes), so this is latent, not live. NOT FIXED HERE by decision — cc-123's Out of scope says file only. The honest fix is a third state rather than counting skipped as a closure: a skip is not evidence the rollup is fresh, so folding it into last_complete_at would be worse than the bug. Candidate shape: carry last_skipped_at + last_skip_reason alongside last_complete_at and suppress escalation (not the report) while the most recent row is a prod_session skip. Cross-ref FIX-950, FIX-1011, FIX-1148, FIX-1135, FIX-973, FIX-944, FIX-1176. MECHANISM BUILT by cc-133 (cd81917c, local); STAYS OPEN for the receipt. Resolved the other way than the candidate shape in this bullet: rather than a third state in check_rollup_freshness, the session now HOLDS the guarded pipelines for its duration and the existing held suppression does the work - so neither check_rollup_freshness nor the FIX-1011 registry changed, and a skip still never counts as a closure. The release clears only holds whose hold_reason carries the prefix 'prod session held: ' (the same prefix the guard procedures write into skip_reason), so a human-declared hold is never cleared by a session and a human hold is never taken over. Because a hold SUPPRESSES instruments, a leftover one is itself a finding: new canary condition prod_session_hold_stale (report tier) fires when session-prefixed holds exist and the lock does not, proved on the clone by SIGKILLing a held claim. CLOSES ON: the same receipt as [[FIX-1172]]. cc-134 (d80a7a28): the instrument for gate (f) of the drain-and-wait block is now a command - pnpm --filter @civitics/data data:census:cancellations:prod reads 57014/min from postgres_logs and front-door 5xx from edge_logs through the Logs API, prints both against their gates, and exits non-zero when either fails. It opens no Postgres connection, which is what makes it readable during the wedge it exists to report. Smoke run 2026-09-18T22:29:15Z to 23:29:15Z: 2 cancellations, 0.0333/min, ratio 1.01x of the 0.033/min baseline, last closed front-door bucket 0 of 1208 = 0.00 percent, both PASS, exit 0 - which independently reproduces cc-129's baseline to two decimal places. The receipt that CLOSES this FIX is unchanged and still Monday's: a supervised hold longer than 48 minutes on prod with no stale-rollup report for financial_entity_totals_refresh and no escalation from the nightly canary. APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-132, the hold ran clean but TOO SHORT to be the receipt — stays open): the FIX-1187 set-2 apply held the box 2026-09-19 18:50:32 to 19:17:25 UTC, 26 m 53 s, against this FIX's close condition of a supervised hold exceeding 48 minutes with no stale-FE report and no escalation. So the receipt did not fire and cannot be claimed. What the run DOES record, all of it consistent with the design: the claim printed 'held 16 guarded pipeline(s) (FIX-1177/1172)' and the release printed 'cleared 16 hold(s)' then 'interlock released'; afterwards prod_session_state() reads held false and label_present false, and rollup_watch_overrides carries 0 rows with the session prefix. The 16 held pipelines correctly deferred rather than ran — entity_connections_rebuild and financial_entity_totals_refresh both logged status skipped at 19:00 and 19:15 with skip_reason 'prod session held: merge-same-person-official-dupes --allow-prod ...', and fe-crawl's first post-release firing at 19:30 logged complete in 0.18 s. One incidental confirmation of the self-healing path this FIX's family relies on: the claim also swept a STALE label left by cc-131's FIX-1195 restore, which had sat since 2026-09-18 02:42:54 — age 141,694 s, 39.4 hours — carrying label_stale true and reason_text 'stale prod_session label with no lock — a dead session; the next claim clears it'. It cleared it. A hold long enough to earn the receipt still needs a landing over 48 minutes; set 2 was deliberately the cheap direction ([[FIX-1192]]) and future manifest-scoped merges are likely to be shorter still, so the receipt may need a different occasion than a merge. No qualifying hold yet (cc-141): no supervised prod session ran, so no skipped firing was produced to observe. Both are two orders of magnitude under the ~48 min threshold at which a skipped firing starts reporting as stale, so this run produced no observation either way and rollup_watch_overrides was not read. The instance this FIX needs is still a long supervised session. The scoped repair between them ran outside the script's session entirely (46 s, 10:17:49-10:18:35). All far under the ~48 min threshold, so no freshness reader was disturbed and this bullet gains nothing. Still waiting on a genuinely long supervised session. <!--id:FIX-1177-->
- 🟡 S — **rule-taggers-daily has been above its 713-858 s band for three consecutive runs (899.5 / 891.2 / 873.3 s) and the filing cc-118 asked for never happened** — Surfaced by [[FIX-1176]]'s FIRST prod run of pnpm receipts:daily (2026-09-12 06:28 UTC) — the band check did exactly what it was built to do. Measured series from cron.job_run_details, jobname rule-taggers-daily (0 6 * * * at 06:30 UTC), twelve consecutive succeeded firings: 09-01 713.3, 09-02 857.9, 09-03 723.8, 09-04 733.6, 09-05 736.5, 09-06 806.9, 09-07 753.7, 09-08 770.3 — those eight ARE the 713-858 band cc-117/cc-118 quote, low and high to the decimal — then 09-09 899.5, 09-10 891.2, 09-11 873.3. THREE CONSECUTIVE ABOVE-BAND RUNS, none of them a failure and none of them near the 6h statement_timeout of [[FIX-969]], so this is creep and not the blowout class. cc-prompt-118 Item 5 made a SECOND consecutive above-band run the filing trigger and named FIX-1170 as the id to use; FIX-1170 was allocated to the NH floterial overlay layer instead, so the trigger fired on 09-10 and the filing was simply lost — which is itself the argument for the receipts file, because a band in a JSON table cannot be forgotten the way a sentence in a prompt can. Not diagnosed here (cc-123 is code-only and its Out of scope forbids changing a band by measurement): the work is to decide whether ~+17% over eight days is proposal-volume growth that should move the band, or the tagger doing more work per proposal that should not. Start from the run's own rows_inserted/rows_updated against the series above. Cross-ref FIX-978, FIX-969, FIX-1152 (the sibling creep watch on the 06:00 daily's search unit), FIX-1176.  APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-135, READ ONLY -- STAYS OPEN, decision pending Craig): the verdict is WORK-PER-ROW, not volume, and the band should NOT move on these numbers. Prod series from data_sync_log (pipeline run_rule_taggers, cadence daily) cross-checked against cron.job_run_details by jobname, 09-01..09-18, eighteen firings: wall 713 -> 945 s (+32.5%) while rows_inserted went 865,299 -> 865,941 (+642 rows, +0.074%). rows_updated is stamped 0 on every daily row -- the procedure only ever writes rows_inserted -- so seconds per 1k rows is the whole story: 0.824 -> 1.091, +32.4%, i.e. essentially all of the wall growth. NOT a drift: two step changes, 770 -> 899 between 09-08 and 09-09 and 898 -> 952 between 09-15 and 09-16, and each step lands on the day AFTER a successful weekly cadence run (09-08 16:00 wrote 4,406,878 size tags; 09-15 16:00 wrote 4,409,537). The 09-01 weekly was reaped at 0 rows and the dailies that week did not step. Both taggers are DELETE-all + INSERT-all against public.entity_tags (rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags clears tag_category='internal' and rewrites ~865k; rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags clears 'size' and rewrites ~4.4M), and neither carries the FIX-943 vacuum tail; entity_tags shows vacuum_count 0 and autovacuum_count 4 lifetime. So the likely unit is the FIX-943/FIX-884 bulk-rewrite bloat shape, and it is a HYPOTHESIS -- the procedure logs no internal phases for the daily cadence (one CALL, one elapsed_seconds stamp), so nothing here separates the DELETE's cost from the FR-join scan's. That separation needs a diagnosis prompt. Also noted, not the same finding: the 09-17 daily ran 7,203.5 s and closed status partial / action canceled, which is the 2h cron_job_budget cancel, not creep. Receipts have read 'above' for seven consecutive days (09-13..09-19 files). docs/receipts/bands.json is deliberately UNTOUCHED.  APPEND 2026-09-19 (cc-136, DIAGNOSED, READ ONLY -- STAYS OPEN, decision pending Craig): the phase split now exists and it REFUTES the cc-135 vacuum-owner hypothesis. Measured on the local prod-clone inside one BEGIN..ROLLBACK, with the function's own proconfig replayed (enable_hashjoin=off, enable_mergejoin=off, work_mem=256MB), on 689,966 internal rows: DELETE 5,835 ms (buffers hit 704,679 / read 16,925), INSERT 117,269 ms of which the FR x votes subtree is 58,792 ms (hit 13,040,603 / read 329,020) and the Insert node's OWN heap+index write is 58,477 ms (hit 20,084,963 / read 820,938 / dirtied 838,458). So of a 123.1 s pass the DELETE is 4.7 percent, the scan 47.7 percent and the index maintenance 47.5 percent. The DELETE is under the 10 percent line the prompt set, so a vacuum owner is not the fix. PROD STATE, entity_tags, read 2026-09-19 02:11:23 and again 02:42:17 UTC (30.9 min apart) and BYTE-IDENTICAL both times, so the between-dailies write rate is zero: n_live_tup 5,359,873, n_dead_tup 71,198 against a 268,044 trigger, n_tup_ins 6,834,279, n_tup_del 7,290,057, autovacuum_count 4, vacuum_count 0, last_autovacuum 2026-09-18 06:47:17 UTC which is 17 minutes AFTER the 06:30 daily, 98.1 percent all-visible, reloptions already 0.05/0.02 from FIX-652. EPOCH CORRECTION: pg_postmaster_start_time is 2026-09-15 15:09:48 UTC and pg_stat_database.stats_reset is NULL, so every cumulative counter above dates from that restart -- cc-135 read vacuum_count 0 / autovacuum_count 4 as LIFETIME and it is not, it is 3.5 days. Four autovacuums in 3.5 days on this table is a healthy cadence, not neglect. WHAT IS ACTUALLY WRONG: entity_tags is BLOATED, not VM-degraded. Heap bloat ratio 1.72 -- relpages 224,824 against 130,340 ideal from sum(avg_width)=172 plus a 24-byte header over 8060 usable bytes per page, i.e. 1,756 MB carrying 1,018 MB of rows, about 738 MB of slack. The same estimator on the freshly restored local clone, whose average row is the SAME width (171), gives 0.93, so the row-mix confound is dead. Indexes are 3,024 MB over that 1,756 MB heap, eight of them, and every DELETE+INSERT cycle rewrites 865k entries in all eight. VACUUM does not fix this: it makes space reusable, it does not compact. OPTION (ii) IS ALSO REFUTED, and on PROD'S OWN PLAN rather than the clone's -- a bare EXPLAIN (no ANALYZE, nothing executed) against prod shows financial_relationships read by a Bitmap Heap Scan via financial_relationships_to, and a bitmap heap scan does not consult the visibility map at all, so FR sitting at 82.7 percent all-visible is irrelevant to this query. The only Index Only Scan anywhere in the plan is on votes via votes_official_voted_at (on the clone: 4,204,255 loops, Heap Fetches 217,660, 13.0M buffer hits, the single largest buffer consumer in the scan subtree), and prod votes is 96.7 percent all-visible with autovacuum_count 0, 13,931 dead and no vacuum owner. If any table's visibility map is worth instrumenting for this job it is votes, not FR. Prod's DELETE plan also differs from the clone's in the cheap direction: prod picks entity_tags_type_category_id with a two-column Index Cond (entity_type, tag_category), where the clone fell back to idx_entity_tags_tag with tag_category as a non-leading filter. So the 4.7 percent figure is an UPPER bound on the DELETE's prod share. THE WEEKLY COUPLING, mechanism named: the daily's cost per row converges onto the weekly's own cost per row. Weekly 09-08 4,692 s / 4,406,878 rows = 1.065 s per 1k, and the dailies after it run 1.030-1.039; weekly 09-15 4,838 s / 4,409,537 = 1.097, and the dailies after it run 1.091-1.100; before the first weekly the daily ran 0.824-0.890. Rows moved +0.074 percent across the window while cost per row moved +32 percent, so what both taggers are paying for is BYTES, not tuples -- the same size-dependent marginal write cost into a table whose physical size each 4.4M-row rewrite ratchets up. Against a 256 MB shared_buffers and a ~10 GB working set, every extra megabyte is a read. That is why it is a step and not a drift: index and heap size do not come back down. PROPOSAL, Craig decides, nothing built here: (a) stop rewriting the whole table -- both taggers DELETE and re-INSERT a set that changed by 642 rows in 18 days, and a merge (INSERT ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING plus a targeted DELETE of only the rows that left the set) removes the index-maintenance phase and the bloat source in one change; (b) a one-off compaction on prod to reclaim the ~738 MB heap slack and the index slack, VACUUM FULL or REINDEX, ACCESS EXCLUSIVE, a supervised window, explicitly NOT this run and not before cc-132; (c) drop idx_entity_tags_visibility, 450 MB at idx_scan 0, and re-examine entity_tags_pkey, 376 MB at idx_scan 0 -- but 3.5 days of counters is too short a window to act on, so the proving read is a second idx_scan sample after a full weekly cycle; (d) stamp delete_seconds / scan_seconds / insert_seconds into the run_rule_taggers data_sync_log metadata so the next creep is diagnosable from the receipts file rather than from a clone replay -- a Monday migration. A vacuum owner is deliberately NOT on this list. WHERE THIS LEAVES THE FIX-943 RULE (recorded here because FIX-943's bullet is archived and has no live bullet to append to): entity_tags is already in that rule's tuned-table list at 0.05/0.02 from FIX-652, autovacuum is firing on time, dead tuples are a quarter of the trigger and the table is 98.1 percent all-visible -- by every instrument that rule watches it is healthy, and it is still 1.72x bloated. The rule buys back dead tuples and the visibility map; it does not buy back PAGES, because VACUUM makes space reusable rather than compacting it. A table rewritten wholesale twice a week needs a compaction answer as well as a vacuum tail, and the rule's own text should say so. docs/receipts/bands.json remains UNTOUCHED. Cross-refs [[FIX-652]], [[FIX-884]], [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-1169]]. APPEND 2026-09-20 (cc-138): PROPOSAL (d) LANDED, AND THIS STAYS OPEN — the instrument is not the fix. run_rule_taggers now stamps metadata.phase_seconds = {delete, insert} in its closing UPDATE, alongside elapsed_seconds. A call to a function is ONE statement and cannot be timed in halves from the caller, so rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags() and rebuild_financial_entity_size_tags() were each SPLIT into a _delete() and an _insert() half, with the original name kept as a two-line wrapper calling both — every other caller (heavy-rebuild.ts, the FIX-1028 sweeps, database.ts) is untouched and keeps its exact contract, return value included, which stays the INSERT's ROW_COUNT. The procedure calls the halves directly inside the SAME transaction with no COMMIT between them, so atomicity is exactly what it was and a cancel still rolls both back. PROCONFIG DECISION: the planner GUCs (enable_hashjoin/mergejoin off) went to the _insert() half ONLY — they exist for the correlated EXISTS over votes, the only join in either half, while the DELETE is three equality predicates on one table, so giving it GUCs it cannot use would be noise that reads as meaningful; size-tags gained none because it had none. The procedure body was taken from PROD and verified byte-identical to 20260911000200:1612-1759 before the edit, and no SET clause was added (it COMMITs). FIRST LOCAL READING, clone, daily cadence: {delete: 7.5, insert: 124.3} of 132 s total over 720,256 tags — THE INSERT IS 94 PERCENT OF THE RUN, which is where (a) should aim. First stamped PROD daily is Monday 06:30 UTC. Note against cc-136: prod's rebuild_pre_vote_timing_tags proconfig does NOT carry work_mem=256MB — the 256MB comes from the plain SET work_mem the PROCEDURE executes before the call and the function inherits. Closes on (a), the temp-table restructure that splits scan from write inside the INSERT. First prod phase_seconds (cc-141): the 2026-09-21 06:30 UTC run stamped delete 46.5 s / insert 947.6 s over a 994 s wall for 868,167 rows — 95.3 percent INSERT, against cc-138's clone prediction of 94.3 percent. The clone got the SHAPE right while understating scale 6-8x (delete 7.5 to 46.5, insert 124.3 to 947.6), which is the reverse of the usual clone caveat and worth keeping. 994 s is the FOURTH consecutive run above the 713-858 s band (09-19 998.9, 09-20 987.3, 09-21 994.0), and the drift is worse than the 899.5 / 891.2 / 873.3 this bullet was filed on. APPEND 2026-09-22 (cc-143, PROPOSAL (a) BUILT AND ON TRUNK -- PRO PENDING, STAYS OPEN): the daily tagger no longer rewrites its category. 20260920080000_fix1178a_pre_vote_timing_merge.sql splits the rebuild into _scan() (CREATE TEMP TABLE pvt_desired AS the desired set, ON COMMIT DROP, every reference pg_temp-qualified so a same-named public table cannot win the search_path), _delete() (current minus desired, plus a 50 percent shrink guard) and _insert() (desired minus current via an anti-join on the UNIQUE key), all three inside ONE transaction with no COMMIT between them, so atomicity is exactly what it was; run_rule_taggers now stamps phase_seconds = {scan, delete, insert} plus desired_rows / deleted_rows, absent rather than null on the weekly branch that measures none of them. MEASURED ON THE CLONE, same clone and cadence as cc-138's reading: the rewrite was {delete: 7.5, insert: 124.3} / 131.8 s / 720,256 rows inserted; the merge is {scan: 16.3, delete: 2.3, insert: 0.5} / 19.2 s cold and {scan: 5.3, delete: 1.1, insert: 0.4} / 6.9 s warm, both at deleted 0 / inserted 0 -- a 6.9x reduction cold. THE RECEIPT THAT MATTERS: across both merge passes entity_tags' heap stayed at 633,126,912 bytes / relpages 77,286, its indexes at 1081 MB and n_dead_tup at 0, where the rewrite left 720,256 dead tuples and 720,256 fresh entries in every index per run. That heap ratchet is what cc-136 named as the actual cause of the creep. TWO THINGS THE DESIGN DID NOT CLAIM, both measured here: (1) a data-writing statement gets NO parallel workers, so the fused INSERT...SELECT was serial BY RULE -- its plan carries no Gather node at all, cost 2,812,079 -- while CREATE TABLE AS is the documented exception and the TEMP CTAS keeps a parallel plan (2 workers, cost 1,447,440) at 4.6 s against 12.96 s for the identical SELECT forced serial, so the split is a PLAN CHANGE and not only an instrument; (2) the planner GUCs are far more load-bearing than assumed and stay, on _scan() alone -- re-measured serially, GUCs off 12.96 s (Bitmap Heap Scan on FR via financial_relationships_to + Index Only Scan on votes_official_voted_at_desc, which is prod's own plan shape per cc-136) against default 228.04 s (a Hash Semi Join that hashes all 969,302 votes then discards 586,340,678 rows on the date-range Join Filter, because a BETWEEN cannot be a hash key), i.e. 17.6x, so design section 4's deferred GUC question is answered in the direction it already pointed and there is NOTHING to file. D5, the source-signature gate, IS NOT BUILT, and it failed on something the prompt's cost gate never tested: HIT RATE. The signature is votes(count,max(updated_at)) || FR(count,max(updated_at)), and on prod over 09-12..09-21 at least one of the two moved on ALL TEN days (FR 7, votes 5, union 10/10), so the gate would never once have fired; its cost was also not free at 2.28 s warm / 14.18 s cold against a 12.96 s scan, because summarising FR requires the same bitmap heap scan of FR the desired set already pays for. THE SHRINK GUARD closes the wrong-but-green shape the rewrite had -- an empty scan deleted the whole category, inserted nothing and reported success -- so _delete RAISEs below 50 percent and the exception lands in the procedure's existing WHEN OTHERS, rolling back with the old state intact. PREDICATES read twice, population UNCHANGED (rule 87): _delete's is today's exactly, because t.tag='pre_vote_timing' sits INSIDE the EXISTS as a condition on the outer row, so a row in this category carrying any other tag has no partner and is deleted as today; and _insert's anti-join keys on the UNIQUE columns and deliberately NOT on generated_by, which is not in that key -- carrying it would collide with a manual row and be swallowed by ON CONFLICT every night. PROD CENSUS 2026-09-22: the category is 868,167 rows, ONE tag (pre_vote_timing), ONE writer (generated_by='rule'), so that asymmetry is a correctness margin rather than a live case. Eleven source anchors plus a double-gated behavioural suite (reachable DB AND CIVITICS_DB_HEAVY_TESTS=1, because it runs the scan three times): desired=720256 d1=1 i1=0 d2=0 i2=0 in 14.2 s -- one planted orphan deleted and nothing else, a foreign-category industry row untouched, the stayer's ORIGINAL row id preserved (spared, not rewritten), a second pass moving nothing, the guard raising on an emptied desired set, and an out-of-order _delete erroring on the missing temp table rather than silently deleting the category. NOT YET ON PRO, NOT YET CLOSED: pnpm db:push:prod is blocked behind rider (c) below, so the first stamped prod merge is the first 06:30 UTC firing after the push and this closes on TWO in-band dailies after that; docs/receipts/bands.json is deliberately UNTOUCHED. The gate opens 2026-09-22 15:09:48 UTC and Craig chose to wait for it rather than ship the merge without the rider, because the merge migration carries the DROP INDEX IF EXISTS twin and pushing first would take ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on a 5.36M-row table on evidence that is not yet valid. The index reads zero because FIX-503 re-pointed the one query shaped for it (snapshot/route.ts fetchEntityTags) at idx_entity_tags_entity, so the zero is a consequence of a fix that landed rather than an accident waiting to be depended on, and no code path names it. LOCAL is already at seven indexes -- the twin ran there. Folded in per rule 70 rather than appended to closed bullets: [[FIX-652]]'s tuned-table entry for entity_tags is untouched, though the table carries SEVEN indexes rather than eight once (c) lands, and [[FIX-1147]]'s tag statistics target is unaffected. Cross-refs [[FIX-884]], [[FIX-943]], [[FIX-1169]], [[FIX-1208]]. - 🟡 S — **Three census functions have no caller anywhere — dead SECURITY DEFINER surface** — Found by the FIX-1128 proconfig census (docs/audits/2026-09-13-fix1128-proconfig-census.md). get_group_donor_totals, has_active_official_grant and rebuild_financial_entity_donation_totals_full are named nowhere: no in-DB caller in pg_proc.prosrc, no repo call site outside the generated packages/db/src/types/database.ts, no pg_trigger use, and zero calls in prod pg_stat_statements since the 2026-09-08 15:12 UTC baseline.
