CANDIDATES, to measure on the clone before choosing (the clone understates scan-bound cost, so quote buffer reads): (1) SET LOCAL max_parallel_workers_per_gather = 0 and a work_mem sized to the box for the stage build, the FIX-1123 remedy; (2) retire the full branch and let the bounded FIX-1112 slice crawl (one atomic slice per unit) catch up from 07-28 with a raised max_units; (3) a supervised one-off bootstrap with the consumers paused. Whichever lands must also stop the FIX-1112 header from asserting a lag-independent 350-420 s. Cross-refs [[FIX-969]] [[FIX-1112]] [[FIX-1123]] [[FIX-1125]] [[FIX-1185]] [[FIX-1194]]. APPEND 2026-09-23 (cc-146, THE FIX IS ON PRO; THE BOOTSTRAP IS NOT RUN, BLOCKED ON [[FIX-1213]]; STAYS OPEN): 20260920100000_fix1212_donor_party_full_rebuild_windowed.sql reached Pro at 02:34:22 UTC (pending 1 to 0; pg_get_functiondef prod against the migration text is 0 lines; proconfig NULL; ACL postgres + service_role). Before it, the prod body was byte-identical to 20260911000100. SHAPE: the full/bootstrap branch is sixteen donor-id windows, each stage (temp table) then DELETE+INSERT of the window then a cursor write, one transaction, COMMIT. Cursor pipeline_state.donor_party_full_rebuild = {mode, started_at, target, windows_done}; target is fixed before window 1; at 16 the watermark goes to target (never backwards) and the cursor is deleted in one transaction; a live cursor wins the mode decision; one older than 7 days is discarded. Guard: stop cleanly before a window if elapsed plus the longest window this call ran would reach unit_budget_seconds (1,500 default; prod has no donor_party_crawl row, so defaults apply), or at max_units. SET max_parallel_workers_per_gather = 0 beside work_mem. The FIX-1112 350-420 s comment is replaced. MEASURED ON THE CLONE, EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS), parallel off, work_mem 256MB: the whole-table stage peaks with HashAggregate 434,193 kB (1,859,976 groups, Batches 1) PLUS a 305,720 kB hash of all 3,680,773 financial_entities, both live, 516,850 buffers; one window peaks with HashAggregate 30,737 kB plus fe hash about 19.2 MB, 223-242k buffers. Bounding only FR is NOT enough: the planner still Seq Scans all of financial_entities into the same 305,720 kB hash per window, so the bound is on fr, ind and fe. THE TRADE: FR has no covering index for this aggregate, so each window touches about 34 pct of FR's heap and sixteen windows read about 7x one pass. PROD PROJECTION: about 340k buffer reads (2.6 GB) and about 70 MB peak per window, about 5.4M reads for all sixteen; wall not projected (the first CALL measures it). CLONE PROOFS (CIVITICS_DB_HEAVY_TESTS=1): full rebuild 16 windows 100.7 s; conservation to the cent against one whole-table aggregate (1,859,976 rows, 693,098,760,800 cents, 4,204,255 tx); max_units 3 capped at [1,2,3] then resumed 4..16 writing the first call's target; a cancel landed inside window 3's apply and left it holding its 115,907 prior rows, with sentinels surviving exactly in un-applied windows. THE BOOTSTRAP IS NOT RUN: prod_session_state() sets defer = held no matter which backend holds the lock, so the prompt's session:claim-prod then CALL logs skipped (proved on local). Craig chose a new prompt adding a claimant opt-in ([[FIX-1213]]) over an unclaimed CALL. WHAT TUESDAY DOES IF 1213 HAS NOT LANDED: the watermark is still 2026-07-28 05:54:28, so Tue 09-29 15:00 takes the full branch, but in the NEW windowed shape: at most about 1,500 s of bounded windows, then partial and resumable, instead of one whole-table statement. Unmeasured on prod, so the pause fallback by Tue 14:00 UTC stays Craig's call. CORRECTION to this bullet: 14.46M is ALL financial_relationships (reltuples 14,457,357), not the donations; the donation-to-official rows the stage aggregates are about 6.2M on prod by the size ratio of financial_relationships_treemap_global_rollup (257 MB against the clone's 174 MB, 4,204,255 rows). [[FIX-1112]] and [[FIX-1123]] are closed and not live: the 1112 claim is corrected in this body and 1123's remedy (parallel off) is applied here. <!--id:FIX-1212-->
- 🔴 S — **The FIX-950 interlock defers the claimant's own CALL — session:claim-prod then CALL of any guarded procedure logs skipped, so no guarded procedure can be run supervised** — Surfaced by cc-146 (2026-09-23) preparing the [[FIX-1212]] supervised bootstrap. prod_session_state() returns defer = held, where held is whether ANY backend holds the prod_supervised_session advisory lock; it never asks which backend. Every guarded pg_cron procedure checks defer before its first write and logs skipped. So the FIX-950 recipe for a supervised run (session:claim-prod, then CALL the procedure) defers the claimant's own CALL. MEASURED on local 2026-09-23: pg_advisory_lock(hashtext('prod_supervised_session')) then CALL public.refresh_donor_party_rollup_incremental() from the SAME backend logged status skipped, skip_reason 'prod session held: (no label)'. The claim script is its own process, so a separate backend is no different. Consequence: no guarded procedure can be run supervised under the interlock that exists to protect supervised runs. The only way today is an unclaimed CALL, which lets every other guarded job (ec-crawl */15 avg 61 s max 721 s, fe-crawl */30, donor-rollup-refresh 09:00/12:00) fire beside it. No earlier cc report ran a guarded procedure under a claim, which is why this was never hit. Blocks the FIX-1212 prod bootstrap, which must land before Tue 2026-09-29 14:00 UTC or Craig takes the pause fallback. Craig's decision 2026-09-23 ~02:45 UTC: a NEW prompt adds an opt-in for the claimant (for example a session-level GUC the guard honours, named in the skip vocabulary so a bypassed defer is visible), rather than an unclaimed run. Cross-refs [[FIX-950]] [[FIX-1212]] [[FIX-1194]]. <!--id:FIX-1213-->
- 🟠 M — **Next 14 caches supabase-js fetches in GET Route Handlers even with force-dynamic — 36 admin-client routes send no no-store signal** — Surfaced by cc-146 closing [[FIX-1208]]. Read from next@14.2.35's own source (server/future/route-modules/app-route/module.js and server/lib/patch-fetch.js), not the docs. In a GET Route Handler, export const dynamic = force-dynamic sets only staticGenerationStore.forceDynamic, and revalidate defaults to false (userland.revalidate ?? false). patch-fetch's auto-no-cache for an Authorization-header or POST fetch fires only when the store's revalidate === 0. So every supabase-js fetch is auto cache with revalidate false, a one-year Data Cache entry keyed on URL+body+headers, UNTIL something earlier in the same request lowers revalidate to 0: a fetch with cache no-store, cookies(), headers(), or noStore(). That is why the cron-watchdog route (its RPC was its only fetch) answered from one cached payload for 17 h while platform-snapshot's RPCs do reach the gateway: supabase-prometheus.ts:629 makes a no-store fetch before its get_supabase_cpu_max RPC at :720. The docs' claim that force-dynamic equals fetchCache force-no-store does not hold for a Route Handler in this version. CENSUS (grep, file level, 2026-09-23): 36 route.ts under apps/civitics/app export GET only, call createAdminClient(), and contain no cookies()/headers()/noStore/no-store; 33 of them export no revalidate. Three are crons: nightly-sync, notify-followers, platform-snapshot (its platform_alert_state reads may run before the no-store fetch). File level is not request order, so each needs its first-fetch order read. Probably also the real mechanism of the FIX-878 stale chord RPC in project memory. REMEDY candidates: per route, createAdminClient({ fetch: noStoreFetch }) (the FIX-1208 shape, shipped 07d193a5) or export const fetchCache = force-no-store; or make no-store the admin client's default inside Next (a behaviour change for ~90 app callers, so enumerate first). Verify each against edge_logs, never against the route's own response. Cross-refs [[FIX-1208]] [[FIX-1194]]. <!--id:FIX-1214-->
- 🟠 M — **Prod-op windows exist only as prose — prod_op_gate() makes gate (g) + (d)/(e)/(f) readable, session:wait-for-gate waits on it, and a landing can run unattended** — Every supervised prod op since cc-131 has been clocked by hand against PROMPT_TEMPLATE.md gates (a)-(g): a prompt restates the nightly start, the 05:45-09:00 blackout, the vacuum spacing (rule 155, cc-141: a fixed 22:30-01:00 band lost a whole run to a nightly that had already finished), the */2 watchdog health and the interlock, and CC computes a clock from them. That makes every landing an attended one, and Craig stated 2026-09-22 that he is asleep before 02:00 PDT and at work 06:30-15:00 PDT, so the [[FIX-1212]] bootstrap (deadline Tue 2026-09-29 14:00 UTC) has no attended window that is also a safe one. Build: (a) public.prod_op_gate(p_expected_seconds, p_now) returns {ok, blocked_by[], readings} from the instruments themselves: nightly running row + next start (22:35 UTC, cc-141), vacuum spacing from cron.job_run_details by NAME (>60 s runs: 90 min after END; any: 10 min; no >60 s-mean daily slot inside [now, now + 2x expected]), the blackout from pipeline_state.ec_crawl (the crawl_gate() parse, [[FIX-1124]]), both */2 watchdogs (runs, wall, startup timeouts, budget overruns), prod_session_state() held/live_writers ([[FIX-950]]), and stuck guarded units; the Logs API census stays in TypeScript (it must be readable when Postgres is not); (b) session:wait-for-gate polls it on a short-lived connection and optionally claims the session and runs a command under the claimant GUC ([[FIX-1213]]); (c) data:donor-party:bootstrap:prod is the first unattended runner built on it: wait, census, claim, CALL until caught_up with a measured stop rule in code, release, receipt. Cross-refs [[FIX-1213]] [[FIX-950]] [[FIX-1212]] [[FIX-1194]] [[FIX-1125]]. <!--id:FIX-1215-->

## COMMUNITY & AUTH
- 🟡 M — **User flagging on data rows — data_flags table + FlagDataButton + admin triage** — Today's flagging stops at user-generated content (comments + civic-initiative arguments via [[FIX-057]]). No mechanism lets a user flag a data row — a wrong vote attribution, a missing source, a misspelled name, an outdated affiliation. Action: (a) new data_flags table — (id, entity_type, entity_id, source, reason ENUM('wrong'|'outdated'|'missing_source'|'misspelled'|'other'), comment TEXT, flagger_user_id REFERENCES users, created_at, classification TEXT NULL, classification_confidence NUMERIC NULL, auto_resolved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL, admin_resolved_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL, resolution ENUM('valid'|'invalid'|'duplicate'|'no_action') NULL). The classification / confidence / auto_resolved_at fields are forward-design slots for a future enrichment_queue auto-moderator (separate FIX, NOT built here) — a Haiku worker reads WHERE classification IS NULL, scores, auto-resolves obvious spam, escalates the rest. (b) FlagDataButton.tsx integrated alongside SourceBadge ([[FIX-399]]), opens a reason picker + optional comment. Auth-required (matches existing content_flags pattern). Rate-limited per-user-per-day. (c) Extend ModerationSection.tsx with a data_flags tab — admin sees WHERE admin_resolved_at IS NULL AND (classification IS NULL OR classification_confidence < 0.8), marks resolved with one of the four resolution values. (d) NOT in scope: the enrichment_queue auto-moderator (separate future FIX), per-source quality scoring, correction-suggestion workflow (Wikipedia-grade — separate initiative, see Citizens Council in PLATFORM_REBUILD_SPEC). Cross-ref [[FIX-057]] (existing comment-flag pattern to mirror), [[FIX-399]] (predecessor SourceBadge for visual placement), [[FIX-397]] (predecessor schema for the entity_type vocabulary). <!--id:FIX-401-->
- 🟢 S — **Notification fan-out on official-claim approve/reject** — Follow-up to [[FIX-559]]: when an admin approves or rejects a pending official claim at /admin/grants, the claimant learns the outcome only by revisiting /profile or the official page. The notifications table exists — wire an approve/reject notification insert in POST /api/admin/grants/[id] (and optionally an auto-approve confirmation in the claim route fast path). Deliberately not built in the FIX-557..559 PR. <!--id:FIX-560-->
- 🟢 L — **jurisdiction_admin / staff / institution_admin claim flows (enum values shipped, no consumer)** — The v2 enum values for jurisdiction_admin, staff (delegation), institution_admin, verified_human and evidence methods id_document/delegation/invitation shipped enum-only in [[FIX-557]]. Claim UIs and grant issuance flows for these roles are deferred until a feature consumes them (per the v2 design decision — don't build gates with no consumer). When the first consumer lands, mirror the official claim flow ([[FIX-558]]) + review queue ([[FIX-559]]) patterns; staff goes through delegated_from rather than direct evidence. <!--id:FIX-561-->
- 🟡 M — **Platform-seed the launch investigation batch (prod live but empty)** — Investigations MVP shipped ([[FIX-577]]..[[FIX-585]]) but prod is live-but-empty: 0 investigations, 0 promoted edges (the PR3 prod rebuild correctly returned 0). Author and promote an initial platform-seeded batch of well-cited launch investigations (`is_seeded=true`, surfaced as featured) that model the citation-discipline standard for early users: every evidence card cites internal records (tier 1) or imported LittleSis/ICIJ entities (tier 2); edge cards corroborated and admin-promoted through the live flow so the graph shows a few real `evidence_source=investigation` edges. Content authoring (pairs with the AI Demonstration Jurisdiction seed-content effort, design-phase2 §2), not code: decide the launch set, author it, then run `create_investigation` / `add_evidence_card` and admin-promote on prod. Cross-ref [[FIX-585]] (promotion flow), `design-investigations-mvp.md` §6. <!--id:FIX-593-->
- 🟠 M — **Evidence-rubric schema (PR A) — investigation_type, measure claim_type, record-level citations, completeness read** — [[FIX-593]] is filed as "content authoring, not code" — that premise is wrong, and it blocks the seed. The evidence-rubric schema from `design-investigation-types-rubric.md` §5 was never filed, so four required slots of the first drafted case (HR 26 / roll-call `2025-house-035`) have nowhere to be written. Scope: (1) `investigations.investigation_type` as a first-class column, not `metadata`, so it can be filtered, indexed and joined to the rubric config (rubric definitions live in code, versioned). (2) `evidence_cards.claim_type` must accept **`measure`** — payload in `metadata` carrying pattern name, parameters, result, denominator, coverage and `computed_at`, so a reader can re-run it. **Measured 2026-07-28: the data holds only `context` and `edge`.** The HR 26 case needs measure cards for `timing`, `comparison`, `specificity` and `tag_composition`, so it is roughly half unwritable until this lands. (3) `citations` must accept a roll-call vote row and a `financial_relationships` row as targets, not just the six searchable entity types; both tables already carry what a receipt needs. (4) A completeness read — required-slots-filled per investigation, derivable from `(investigation_type, evidence_cards.claim_type + metadata.slot)` — which drives display, a sort key and the review gate. **Two decisions to settle inside this PR, not before it:** (a) `financial_relationships.fec_filing_id` is listed as a cited field in the `donations` slot but is **NULL on all 4,459,559 donation rows**, and `source_url` is committee-level (9,257 distinct URLs across those rows) so it resolves the donor, not the donation — either drop the field from the slot definition or block on populating it; (b) the R8 topical-fit affirmation currently has no home, and the proposed `metadata.affirmed_by` + `metadata.affirmed_at` is an untyped JSON convention with nothing enforcing it — decide whether a typed column or a check constraint is warranted while there are 2 investigations rather than 200. Do NOT rewire the corroboration trigger in this PR (rubric §5): the meaningful corroboration for a measure card is that the computation still reproduces, so let it carry a re-verification timestamp instead. Also fold the reader-facing register rules into the type definition — the `topical_rationale` card and the claim are the only two blocks an ordinary visitor reads, and they must be plain language; see `case-hr26-draft-2026-07-29.md` §10 for the worked example. Cross-ref [[FIX-593]], [[FIX-585]]. <!--id:FIX-927-->
- 🟠 S — **Revoking an ACTIVE grant still tells the holder nothing** — FIX-560 wired a claim_outcome notification into approve, reject and the exact-email auto-approve path. The THIRD action on POST /api/admin/grants/[id] — revoke (FIX-928), which withdraws live access rather than deciding a pending claim — was out of its scope and still writes entity_grants plus grant_events and says nothing to the account losing access. That is the same silence FIX-560 removed, on the action with the highest stakes: the holder keeps using a profile they no longer control until something 403s. revoke_grant() is SET-BASED on the (user_id, role, target_type, target_id) key and returns the count, so one click can retire several rows and the notification has to be per affected user, not per grant id — read the revoked set back rather than assuming one. buildClaimOutcomeNotification in apps/civitics/src/lib/claim-notification.ts already has the shape; it needs a third outcome ('revoked') and its own title, since 'Your claim was not approved' is wrong for access that WAS approved and has now ended. See FIX-560 and FIX-928. <!--id:FIX-1205-->
